The [Gaza Strip](12047.md) is experiencing a [humanitarian crisis](1251700.md) as a result of the [Gaza war](74998519.md) and [genocide](75668427.md).[^cite_note-spiraling_humanitarian_crisis-1][^cite_note-humanitarian_catastrophe-2] The crisis includes both an ongoing [famine](75828824.md) and a [healthcare collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse). At the start of the [war](33158.md), Israel tightened its [blockade on the Gaza Strip](75013719.md), which has resulted in significant shortages of fuel, food, medication, water, and essential medical supplies.[^cite_note-spiraling_humanitarian_crisis-1][^cite_note-3] This siege resulted in a 90% drop in electricity availability, impacting hospital power supplies, sewage plants, and shutting down the desalination plants that provide drinking water.[^cite_note-4] Doctors warned of disease outbreaks spreading due to overcrowded hospitals.[^cite_note-humanitarian_catastrophe-2] According to [a United Nations special committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People),[^cite_note-UNCommitteeHeadGenocide-5] [Amnesty International](18947898.md), and other experts and human rights organisations, Israel [has committed genocide](75668427.md) against the [Palestinian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people) during [its ongoing invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) and [bombing of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-genocide-6][^cite_note-genocide_int_law_scholars-7]

Heavy bombardment by Israeli airstrikes caused catastrophic damage to Gaza’s infrastructure, further deepening the crisis.[^cite_note-8] The [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) reported over 4,000 children killed in the war's first month.[^cite_note-9] UN Secretary General [António Guterres](471806.md) stated Gaza had "become a graveyard for children."[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13][^cite_note-14] In May 2024, the [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAID) head [Samantha Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samantha_Power) stated that conditions in Gaza were "worse than ever before".[^cite_note-15]

Organizations such as [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md), the [Red Cross](15487.md), and a joint statement by [UNICEF](19698110.md), the World Health Organization, the [UN Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Development_Programme), [United Nations Population Fund](63952.md), and [World Food Programme](33582.md) have warned of a dire humanitarian collapse.[^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] In early March 2025, Israel began a complete blockade of all food and supplies going into Gaza, ending only in late May with limited distribution by the controversial [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md). Since then, [many aid-seekers have been killed](80075155.md) or wounded while trying to obtain food.&#x20;

As of August 2025, [Integrated Food Security Phase Classification](16431152.md) (IPC) projections show 100% of the population are experiencing "high levels of acute food insecurity", and 32% are projected to face Phase 5 catastrophic levels by September 30, 2025.[^cite_note-IPC_22_August_2025-19] On 22 August 2025, the IPC said that famine is taking place in one of the five [governorates](5811262.md) in the Gaza Strip: specifically, the [Gaza Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Governorate) which includes [Gaza City](55387.md). The IPC added that, within the next month, famine was likely to occur in the [Deir al-Balah Governorate](14704596.md) and [Khan Yunis Governorate](13900254.md). The IPC had insufficient data on the [North Gaza Governorate](8689663.md) for a classification but concluded that conditions were likely similar or worse than in the Gaza Governorate.[^cite_note-IPC_22_August_2025-19][^cite_note-reuters20250822-20] Within the next 6 weeks as of 16 August, the number of people in IPC Phase 5 is expected to rise from 500,000 to over 640,000.[^cite_note-21]

## Food and water

### Famine

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Gaza Strip famine](75828824.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_Strip_famine\&action=edit)]

The population of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) underwent a [famine](51329.md) as a result of an [Israeli blockade](75013719.md) during the [Gaza war](74998519.md) between 22 August 2025 and 19 December 2025.[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_reuters20251219-23] The prevention of basic essentials and [humanitarian aid](76066918.md) from entering Gaza,[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_un20240318-26] as well as [airstrikes](76139149.md) that destroyed food infrastructure, such as bakeries, mills, and food stores, caused a widespread scarcity of essential supplies.[^cite_note-28] Humanitarian aid was also blocked by [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_blockade_of_aid_delivery_to_the_Gaza_Strip) at borders and ports. Increasing [societal breakdown in Gaza](77392232.md), including looting, was also cited as a barrier to the provision of aid.

By August 2025, [Integrated Food Security Phase Classification](16431152.md) (IPC) projections showed that 100% of the population are experiencing "high levels of acute food insecurity", and 32% were projected to face Phase 5 catastrophic levels by 30 September 2025.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_IPC_22_August_2025-29] On 22 August 2025, the IPC said that famine is taking place in at least one of the five [governorates](5811262.md) in the Gaza Strip: specifically, the [Gaza Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Governorate) which includes [Gaza City](55387.md). The IPC added that, within the next month, famine was likely to also occur in both the [Deir al-Balah Governorate](14704596.md) and the [Khan Yunis Governorate](13900254.md). The IPC had insufficient data on the [North Gaza Governorate](8689663.md) for a classification but concluded that conditions were likely similar or worse than in the Gaza Governorate.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_IPC_22_August_2025-29][^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_reuters20250822-30] Within the next 6 weeks as of 16 August, the number of people in IPC Phase 5 was expected to rise from 500,000 to over 640,000.[^cite_note-31]

On 22 October 2025, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) found that Israel is obliged to guarantee sufficient food to Gazans and to allow [UNRWA](32270.md) to operate in Gaza.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_ICJ_2025Oct22_obligations_IL-32] On 19 December, the IPC published a report declaring that there is no longer a famine in Gaza after improvements in food delivery following the ceasefire, but warned that the situation remains "highly fragile" with 100,000 still facing "catastrophic conditions", although that number was projected to decline to 1,900 by April 2026.[^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_reuters20251219-23][^cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_apnews20251219-33] As of January 2026, Gazans have received enough humanitarian aid to meet their basic food needs for the first time since the start of the war.[^cite_note-34]

However, in late July 2026, the IPC reported that the scale-up of humanitarian assistance, enabled by the October 2025 ceasefire, has driven improvements in food security and nutrition conditions across Gaza, but that over 1.2 million people (59% of the population of Gaza) continued to face Phase 3 Crisis levels of food insecurity or worse, including 212,000 (about 10% of Gazans) experiencing Phase 4 Emergency levels, and warned that Gaza is still experiencing a severe food emergency that could develop into another famine.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36]

The [ceasefire](81215079.md) between Israel and Hamas went into effect on 10 October 2025.[^cite_note-39] Under the ceasefire terms, Israel was to permit up to 600 humanitarian aid trucks to enter Gaza each day. Since then, the limit has been reduced to 300, with Israeli officials attributing the change to delays in recovering the bodies of Israeli hostages believed to be buried beneath rubble from Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-onworf-40] Data from the UN2720 Monitoring and Tracking Dashboard, which records the movement of humanitarian convoys entering Gaza, showed that between 10 and 16 October, only 216 trucks reached their intended destinations within Gaza.[^cite_note-onworf-40]

### Water supply

See also: [Water supply and sanitation in Palestine](21378125.md) and [Israeli airstrikes on municipal services in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrikes_on_municipal_services_in_the_Gaza_Strip)



Boy filling a water container with a [garden hose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_hose) in late-October 2023

Before the war, Gaza purchased a small share of its water from Israel (6% in 2021).[^cite_note-41] Israel's blockade of water pipelines exacerbated [water supply issues in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_the_State_of_Palestine), which already had a near lack of fit-to-drink [aquifers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquifers).[^cite_note-Akjsdfajkhasdkjfh-42] On 12 October, the United Nations said that Israeli actions had caused water shortages affecting 650,000 people.[^cite_note-ap8-43] On 14 October, [UNRWA](32270.md) announced Gaza no longer had clean drinking water, and two million people were at risk of death.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-collapse-45]

On 16 October, Israel claimed to have provided water near southern [Khan Younis](101330.md), but the Gaza Interior Ministry denied this.[^cite_note-46] By the same time, residents were drinking [seawater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seawater) and [brackish water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackish_water) from farm wells, raising fears of [waterborne diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterborne_diseases).[^cite_note-drinking_salty_water-47][^cite_note-water-dwindle-48] Doctors and hospital staff drank [IV solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intravenous_therapy).[^cite_note-49] By 17 October, the UN stated Gaza's last [seawater desalination plant](156787.md) had shut down.[^cite_note-water_access-50] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* stated fears were growing people had begun to die from dehydration.[^cite_note-Akjsdfajkhasdkjfh-42] On 18 October, Israel announced it would not allow fuel to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-Joe_Biden_warns_Israel-51] The UNRWA stated fuel was needed to resume water pump operations.[^cite_note-Joe_Biden_warns_Israel-51] Some Gazans purchased water from private vendors who purified water with [solar panels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panel).[^cite_note-52] On 19 October, the UN reported Gazans were surviving on a daily average of three liters of water each.[^cite_note-53] The [World Health Organization](33583.md) recommends a minimum of 50 to 100 liters per day.[^cite_note-water_access-50] On 22 October, the UN stated Gazans had resorted to drinking [dirty water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contaminated_water).[^cite_note-54]

In November 2023, the UN stated many still relied on "brackish or saline ground water," if they were drinking any water at all.[^cite_note-Lederer-55] On 6 November, [OCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCHA) stated continued water shortages were raising fears of dehydration.[^cite_note-56] UNRWA announced on 15 November that due to the lack of fuel, 70 percent of Gaza would no longer have access to clean water.[^cite_note-57] On 17 November, [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated Gaza's water supply was at seventeen percent of its pre-siege capacity.[^cite_note-58] On 27 November, residents in northern Gaza received their first aid delivery of clean water since the war began.[^cite_note-59]

[Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated on 18 December 2023 the water system in Gaza had collapsed.[^cite_note-60] [UNICEF](19698110.md) reported children in southern Gaza were receiving 1.5 liters of water a day, while the minimum amount for survival is 3 liters per day.[^cite_note-61] As of December 6, the sole water desalination facility in northern Gaza was inoperative, while the pipeline that delivers water from Israel to the north remained shut, thereby heightening the likelihood of dehydration and waterborne illnesses due to the consumption of unsafe water sources. The impact on hospitals has been severe, as only one out of the 24 hospitals in northern Gaza is operational and capable of accepting new patients, albeit with limited services, as of December 14.[^cite_note-HRW-2023-12-18-62] On 13 December, Israel began pumping [seawater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seawater) into tunnels reportedly used by Hamas.[^cite_note-63] Experts warned this would irreversibly damage Gaza's [water aquifers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquifer) and clearwater supply.[^cite_note-64] The IDF acknowledged it was flooding the tunnels on 30 January.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66]

In January 2024, the Israeli army destroyed Gaza City’s main reservoirs, Al-Balad and Al-Rimal.[^cite_note-67] The director of ambulance and emergency centres for Gaza stated on 20 January that the "struggle for water is a daily torment".[^cite_note-68] The UNOCHA director for Gaza stated, "We can only meet a third of the population’s need for clean drinking water."[^cite_note-69]

In February 2024, the [Food and Agriculture Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization) stated water was at 7 percent of pre-October levels.[^cite_note-70] In May 2024, the [Coastal Municipalities Water Utility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_Municipalities_Water_Utility) reported, "The entire water supply and sewage management systems are nearing total collapse because the damage is so extensive".[^cite_note-71] UNOCHA stated some displaced people were surviving on 3 percent of minimum daily water needs.[^cite_note-72] UNICEF made a deal with Israel in June 2024 to restore a desalination plant.[^cite_note-73]

In July 2024, the Israeli military stated it had allowed power to be restored to a desalination plant in Gaza.[^cite_note-74] Children in Al-Mawasi were waiting six to eight hours a day looking for water, with officials stating facilities serving as many as 700,000 people were out of service.[^cite_note-75] By August 2024, UNRWA warned that Gaza's water crisis had grown more severe, with people only receiving about half of the required liters of water per day.[^cite_note-76] Children were reported to be drinking from puddles due to the lack of available drinking water.[^cite_note-77]

## Disease

### Physical health and disease

See also: [2024 Gaza Strip polio epidemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Gaza_Strip_polio_epidemic)

| Avatar of World Health OrganizationAvatar of World Health Organization | **World Health Organization** (@WHO) tweeted: |
| --- | --- |

> As deaths and injuries in Gaza continue to rise due to intensified hostilities, intense overcrowding and disrupted health, water, and sanitation systems pose an added danger: the rapid spread of infectious diseases.

November 8, 2023[^cite_note-78]

Public health experts warned of the outbreak and spread of disease in Gaza. According to [Oxfam](19698439.md) and the United Nations, Gaza's lack of clean water and [sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_management) would trigger a rise in [cholera](7591.md) and other deadly [infectious diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infection).[^cite_note-Gaza’s_next_big_threat-79] Oxfam stated Gaza's [sewage pumping stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sewage_treatment) and [wastewater treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wastewater_treatment) facilities had ceased operations, so the buildup of solid waste and [unburied bodies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_risks_from_dead_bodies) were likely vectors of disease.[^cite_note-Gaza’s_next_big_threat-79] Due to the lack of [clean drinking water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drinking_water), Gaza residents were drinking water contaminated with [sewage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sewage), [seawater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seawater), and [farm water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farm_water), another major source of disease.[^cite_note-Gaza’s_next_big_threat-79] Richard Brennan, regional emergency director at [WHO](33583.md), stated, "The conditions are ripe for the spread of a number of diarrhoeal and skin diseases".[^cite_note-Gaza’s_next_big_threat-79]

Doctors also warned of overcrowded conditions at schools and hospitals. Dr. Nahed Abu Taaema stated overcrowded shelters were "a prime breeding ground for disease to spread".[^cite_note-Overcrowding,_poor_sanitation-80] Abu Taaema reported a rise in rashes, lung infections, and stomach issues.[^cite_note-Overcrowding,_poor_sanitation-80] On 24 October, the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) recorded 3,150 cases of disease from drinking [contaminated water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_pollution), mostly among children.[^cite_note-81] The lack of [medical supplies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_supplies) was another reported issue, as the World Health Organization reported a sanitation crisis in hospitals, with some struggling to sanitize [surgical equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surgical_instrument).[^cite_note-82] Dr. Iyad Issa Abu Zaher stated, "The outbreak of disease is inevitable".[^cite_note-Gaza_kidney-83] UNRWA schools, where an estimated 600,000 Gazans were sheltering, reported outbreaks of [scabies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scabies) and [chicken pox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicken_pox), as well as a lack of basic hygiene for women [menstruating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_cycle).[^cite_note-84] On 27 October, [Action Against Hunger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Against_Hunger) warned people were developing [kidney failure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidney_failure) due to the consumption of [salt water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salinity) and [dehydration](155056.md).[^cite_note-AAH-Oct27-85]

On 6 November, [OCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCHA) stated [individuals with disabilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disability) were suffering disproportionately due to the lack of accommodations in most shelters.[^cite_note-86] UNRWA announced cases of respiratory infections, diarrhoea and chicken pox had been reported at its shelters.[^cite_note-87] On 10 November, WHO stated infectious diseases, including diarrhea and chickenpox, were soaring across the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-88] OCHA stated [accumulated waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_management) in the streets risked the spread of airborne diseases and infestations of [insects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insect) and [rats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rat).[^cite_note-89] Doctors reported that due to a lack of fresh water and [iodine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iodine), patients wounds were often infested with [maggots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maggot).[^cite_note-90]

Raw sewage overflowed in the streets, creating a health and [environmental disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_disaster).[^cite_note-91] On 8 November, the World Health Organization stated that since the start of the conflict, 33,551 cases of diarrhea had been reported, 8,944 cases of [scabies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scabies) and [lice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lice), 1,005 cases of chickenpox, 12,635 cases of [skin rash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skin_rash) and 54,866 cases of [upper respiratory infections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_respiratory_infection).[^cite_note-92] On 17 November, WHO updated these numbers, stating there were 70,000 cases of acute respiratory infections and over 44,000 cases of diarrhea, which were significantly higher than expected.[^cite_note-93] [UNICEF](19698110.md) warned the worsening sanitation situation threatened a mass disease outbreak.[^cite_note-94]

On 28 November, WHO stated more Palestinians risked dying from disease than bombings.[^cite_note-95] UNRWA chief [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) warned of an impending humanitarian "tsunami" as people succumbed to disease and the deprivation of sanitation and clean water.[^cite_note-96] A [Hepatitis A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis_A) outbreak was reported by the United Nations on 3 December.[^cite_note-97] The UN reported disease outbreaks in southern Gaza shelters.[^cite_note-98] [Volker Türk](71721726.md) warned of unsanitary conditions amidst mass displacement in southern Gaza.[^cite_note-99] On 7 December, the World Health Organization reported increases in acute respiratory infections, scabies, [jaundice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaundice), and diarrhea.[^cite_note-100] On 13 December, 360,000 cases of infectious diseases were reported in shelters.[^cite_note-101] On 20 December, WHO reported Gaza was experiencing "soaring rates of infectious disease outbreaks".[^cite_note-102]

On 29 December, WHO chief Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus reported 180,000 cases of upper respiratory infections, 136,400 cases of diarrhoea, 55,400 cases of lice and scabies, 5,330 cases of chickenpox, 42,700 cases of skin rash, and 4,722 cases of [impetigo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impetigo).[^cite_note-103] Flooding in Gaza spread sewage water, raising fears of the spread of disease.[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] On 19 January, [Yahya Al-Sarraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Al-Sarraj), the [mayor of Gaza City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Gaza_City), stated more than 50,000 tons of trash had accumulated in the city, further leading to the spread of disease.[^cite_note-106] Parents reported children falling sick after being exposed to raw sewage.[^cite_note-107]

In his address to the UN Security Council on 31 January 2024, Martin Griffiths, the UN under-secretary general for humanitarian affairs and emergency relief coordination, provided a comprehensive overview of the challenges currently confronting numerous individuals in Gaza. According to our latest estimates, approximately 75 percent of the entire population has been displaced. The living conditions they endure are deplorable and deteriorating with each passing day. The makeshift tent camps, established by refugees and displaced people, are being inundated by heavy rains, compelling children, parents, and the elderly to seek shelter in the mud. The issue of food insecurity continues to escalate, while access to clean water remains almost entirely unattainable. Given the limited availability of public health support, preventable diseases are rampant and will persistently propagate.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_impact_of_the_conflict-108] On 4 March, the Gaza Health Ministry stated they had recorded about one million cases of infectious diseases.[^cite_note-109]

The situation in Gaza is worsening, leading to the spread of diseases due to the lack of clean water and insufficient sewage facilities. According to the United Nations, the people in Gaza are facing a shortage of water and hygiene materials, which is negatively impacting their overall well-being and physical health. Additionally, the accumulation of solid waste in public areas, hospitals, IDP shelters, and other locations is a major concern as it poses significant risks to public health. The uncollected waste, amounting to tens of thousands of tons, is exacerbating these risks.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_impact_of_the_conflict-108] In April 2024, the Gaza Media Office stated environmental contamination in northern Gaza had reached "unprecedented levels" due to "mountains of waste and hundreds of mass graves".[^cite_note-110] In May 2024, the UN stated, "Mosquitoes, flies and rats are spreading, and so are diseases."[^cite_note-111] Oxfam reported the threat of disease outbreaks due to an accumulation of "human waste and rivers of sewage in the streets".[^cite_note-112]



Polio vaccines being administered at an UNRWA shelter in [Al-Shati refugee camp](16296150.md), the Gaza Strip, on 22 February 2025

In July 2024, [poliovirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poliovirus) was detected in Gaza's sewage water.[^cite_note-113] On 29 July, The Gaza Health Ministry officially declared a [polio epidemic in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Gaza_Strip_polio_epidemic).[^cite_note-\:32-114]

According to a letter sent to President [Joseph R. Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_R._Biden), Vice President [Kamala D. Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_D._Harris), and others on October 2, 2024 by 99 American healthcare workers who have served in the Gaza Strip since October 7, 2023, and cited in a study from the [Watson Institute for International and Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watson_Institute_for_International_and_Public_Affairs) at [Brown University](4157.md), at least 5,000 people in Gaza have died due to a lack of access to care for chronic diseases according to a conservative estimate.[^cite_note-MotherJonesTotalDeaths-115][^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-116][^cite_note-GazaHealthcareLettersEstimatedDeaths-117]

### Psychological health

See also: [Mental health in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mental_health_in_Palestine)



Displacement, loss, and violence brought about by the war have contributed to worsening mental health amongst Palestinians in Gaza.

[^cite_note-118]

Weeks of continuous air strikes and explosions have contributed to the psychological destruction of children in Gaza. Following 16 days of bombardment, children developed severe trauma, with symptoms including convulsion, aggression, bed-wetting, and nervousness. 90% of children in pediatric hospitals in Gaza exhibited or reported symptoms of anxiety, the majority exhibited post-traumatic stress symptoms, and 82% reported fears of imminent death.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] On 6 November, [UNICEF](19698110.md) spokesman Toby Fricker warned of the psychological impacts and "massive stress" experienced by children in Gaza.[^cite_note-121] On 17 November, WHO chief [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md) stated 20,000 people were in need of specialized [mental health services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Services_for_mental_disorders).[^cite_note-122] On 9 January 2024, [OCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCHA) reported 485,000 people with mental health issues were experiencing care disruption.[^cite_note-UNOCHA-123]

## Healthcare

Main article: [Attacks on health facilities during the Gaza war](75314000.md)

See also: [Healthcare in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Palestine) and [Timeline of the Gaza Strip healthcare collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse)



Doctor with wounded child, [Al-Shifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_hospital)

The [healthcare system of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Palestine) faced several humanitarian crises as a result of the conflict. Due to Israel's siege, hospitals faced a lack of fuel and relied on [backup generators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backup_generator) for the first two weeks of the war.[^cite_note-124] By 23 October, Gaza hospitals began shutting down as they ran out of fuel, starting with the [Indonesia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Hospital).[^cite_note-125] When hospitals lost power completely, multiple [premature babies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_Strip_preterm_births) in [NICUs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neonatal_intensive_care_unit) died.[^cite_note-abcnews.go.com-126][^cite_note-ABC_News-127] Numerous medical staffers were killed by Israeli airstrikes, and ambulances, health institutions, medical headquarters, and multiple hospitals were destroyed.[^cite_note-Healthcare_destroyed-128] The [Medecins Sans Frontieres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medecins_Sans_Frontieres) said scores of ambulances and medical facilities were damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-ajmedic-130] By late-October, the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) stated the healthcare system had "totally collapsed".[^cite_note-131]

By 5 January 2024, the World Health Organization reported there had been 304 attacks on healthcare facilities in Gaza since 7 October, with 606 deaths.[^cite_note-132] On 24 January, WHO stated seven out of 24 hospitals remained partially operational in Northern Gaza, and seven out of 12 in Southern Gaza.[^cite_note-133] On 26 January, a senior [OHCHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHCHR) official stated, "I fear that many more civilians will die. The continued attacks on specially protected facilities, such as hospitals, will kill civilians".[^cite_note-134] The same day, a [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) coordinator stated, "There is no longer a healthcare system in Gaza."[^cite_note-135] A senior technical adviser with the [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) stated, "There’s nothing that could have prepared me for the horrors that I saw."[^cite_note-136] In May 2024, the [UN Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Development_Programme) stated the conflict could reduce levels of health back to 1980 levels.[^cite_note-137]

### Supplies shortages

Following the [shutdown of the Gaza Strip power station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_crisis_in_the_Gaza_Strip) on 11 October, it was reported that hospitals in Gaza would soon run out of available fuel to power generators.[^cite_note-138] The Al-Aqsa Martyrs Hospital faced a [dialysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidney_dialysis) crisis, with hundreds sharing only 24 dialysis machines.[^cite_note-Gaza_kidney-83] WHO announced it could no longer resupply al-Shifa and al-Quds hospitals due to the high levels of risk.[^cite_note-139] In November, nearly half of all hospitals were out of service due to shortages of fuel and power, and [amputations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amputation) and [C-sections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarean_section) were performed without anesthetic due to shortages of supplies.[^cite_note-140]

On 8 November, Al-Quds completely ran out of fuel and shut down most services.[^cite_note-141] On 13 November, Kamal Adwan Hospital ran out of fuel.[^cite_note-142] The al-Amal Hospital's only generator shut down.[^cite_note-143]

On 6 December, Doctors Without Borders stated fuel and medical supplies at al-Aqsa hospital were critically low.[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] Doctors at [Nasser Medical Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Medical_Complex) in [Khan Younis](101330.md) described a lack of supplies and barely any medical functionality.[^cite_note-146]

From 11 October 2023 onwards, the Gaza Strip has experienced a complete lack of electricity due to the Israeli authorities discontinuing the power supply and depleting the fuel reserves for Gaza's only power plant. The United Nations has observed that this ongoing blackout, along with the shutdown of communications and industrial fuel, is greatly impeding the aid community's ability to assess and effectively address the worsening humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_impact_of_the_conflict-108]

### Attacks and destruction

Main articles: [Attacks on health facilities during the Gaza war](75314000.md) and [Killing of health workers in the Gaza war](75657910.md)

| **Doctors Without Borders** (@MSF_canada) tweeted: |
| --- |

> "We're on the fourth floor, there's a sniper who attacked four patients inside the hospital. One of them has a gunshot wound directly in his neck, and he is a quadriplegic \[patient], and the other one \[was shot] in the abdomen."

November 11, 2023[^cite_note-147]

On 14 October, the Diagnostic Cancer Treatment Centre of the [Al-Ahli Arab Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital) was partially destroyed by Israeli rocket fire.[^cite_note-148] In a statement on 15 October, the [World Health Organization](33583.md) stated four hospitals were no longer functioning after being targeted by Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-149] On 17 October, a widely condemned [explosion in the al-Ahli courtyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion) resulted in significant fatalities.[^cite_note-Reuters-hosp-150]

On 30 October, the [Turkish-Palestinian Friendship Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish-Palestinian_Friendship_Hospital) was severely damaged by an Israeli airstrike.[^cite_note-151] On 9 November, the Gaza government media office stated Israel had bombed eight hospitals in the past three days.[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153]

Israeli tanks surrounded four hospitals, [al-Rantisi Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rantisi_Hospital), al-Nasr Hospital, and the eye and mental health hospitals, from all directions.[^cite_note-154] The Nasser Rantissi paediatric cancer hospital caught on fire after being hit by an Israeli airstrike and began evacuations.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] At least three hospitals were hit by Israeli airstrikes, leading the director of the Al-Shifa hospital to state, "Israel is now launching a war on Gaza City hospitals."[^cite_note-157] The strikes resulted in multiple casualties.[^cite_note-158] The [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent) claimed Israeli snipers opened fire on children at al-Quds hospital, killing one and wounding 28.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161]

On 20 November, Israel launched an offensive on [Indonesia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Hospital) with an airstrike that reportedly killed 12 people.[^cite_note-162] Following the strike, Israeli tanks surrounded the hospital.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] Staff at the hospital reported Israeli soldiers shooting inside the hospital indiscriminately.[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-171] Four doctors were reported killed after Israel bombed al-Awda Hospital on 22 November.[^cite_note-172] The Kamal Adwan hospital stated Israeli bombings increased around the hospital.[^cite_note-173]

On 25 November, the director general of the Ministry of Health stated the Israeli military shot at medical teams during the [temporary ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire) in effect.[^cite_note-174] The director of the [European Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_European_Hospital) stated its paramedics had been wounded in Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-175] On 11 December, MSF stated one of its doctors inside Al Awda Hospital had been injured by an Israeli sniper.[^cite_note-176]

By 18 January 2024, none of Gaza's hospitals remained fully operational.[^cite_note-177] On 19 January 2024, the Jordanian government reported that the Israeli military had deliberately targeted its new field hospital in [Khan Younis](101330.md), using a tank to block the hospital entrance and shooting at the hospital and bunker shelters.[^cite_note-178] On 24 January, the World Health Organization stated it had recorded a total of 660 Israeli attacks on healthcare facilities.[^cite_note-179]

According to United Nations Secretary-General António Guterres, the hostilities in Gaza and Israel have “created appalling human suffering, physical destruction and collective trauma across Israel and the Occupied Palestinian Territory”.[^cite_note-Humanitarian_impact_of_the_conflict-108]

#### Al-Shifa Hospital

Main article: [Al-Shifa Hospital siege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_Hospital_siege)

In early November, Al-Shifa Hospital in Gaza experienced a surge in Israeli attacks, with the facility being bombed five times in 24 hours.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] Families attempting to leave the complex were reportedly shot and killed.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-184]

[Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel) documented the deaths of two premature babies at Al-Shifa due to electricity shortages.[^cite_note-185] IDF spokesperson [Daniel Hagari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Hagari) pledged assistance in evacuating babies, but the Gaza Health Ministry asserted a lack of provided mechanisms.[^cite_note-washingtonpost.com-186][^cite_note-aje.io-187] On November 12, the hospital's director-general stated that 650 patients at Al-Shifa were in danger due to the catastrophic situation, including the destruction of the cardiac ward.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]

Doctors Without Borders reported dire conditions at Al-Shifa Hospital, citing a lack of essentials like food, water, and electricity, with reports of a sniper targeting patients.[^cite_note-A_call_from_Al-Shifa-190] Israel's raid on the hospital on 15 November was described as an unimaginable nightmare.[^cite_note-193] Witnesses stated that Israel did not provide aid or supplies.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] The hospital faced challenges, including decomposing bodies and maggot-infested wounds, due to a lack of essential resources.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-FT_2023-11-16-197]

Amid deteriorating conditions, an [evacuation](1536572.md) of Al-Shifa began on November 18.[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] Ismail al-Thawabta, a Palestinian media office spokesperson, asserted that patients moved to other facilities faced a perilous fate.[^cite_note-201] [ActionAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ActionAid) characterized the evacuation as a death sentence.[^cite_note-202] Concerns were raised about the adequacy of aid. The World Health Organization and the Palestinian Red Crescent participated in evacuation plans, aiming to transfer patients to alternative medical facilities.

The hospital stated six doctors would remain behind with 120 patients too sick to be transferred.[^cite_note-203] A humanitarian team from the [World Health Organization](33583.md) visited al-Shifa and found a lack of food, water, or medicine, with signs of gunfire and a [mass grave](1349803.md).[^cite_note-204] The director of al-Shifa said people were only given one hour to evacuate, stating, "we were forced to leave at gunpoint."[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206] WHO stated 25 health workers and 291 patients, including 32 babies remained at al-Shifa.[^cite_note-207]

On 19 November, the premature babies at al-Shifa were evacuated to southern Gaza, where they were planned to be moved to Egypt the following day.[^cite_note-apnews.com-208] The World Health Organization stated it was planning missions to transport the remaining al-Shifa patients to [Nasser Medical Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Medical_Complex) and [European Gaza Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Gaza_Hospital) in the next 2–3 days.[^cite_note-209]

Staff at Al-Shifa stated 50 patients, including infants, had died due to power and oxygen shortages.[^cite_note-210] The director of Al-Shifa stated Israel's claim to provide [incubators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incubator_\(neonatal\)) to premature babies was false.[^cite_note-Israel's_claims_of_providing_incuba-211] On 22 November, the Palestinian Red Crescent stated fourteen ambulances had arrived at al-Shifa to evacuate the hospital's remaining patients.[^cite_note-212]

On 26 November, the conditions for remaining patients at al-Shifa were reportedly dire.[^cite_note-213] On 27 November, the Ministry of Health reported a volunteer effort at al-Shifa hospital sought to restart the dialysis department.[^cite_note-214] By 28 November, the dialysis unit was reportedly reopened and receiving patients.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] On 7 December, the Gaza Health Ministry stated only basic [first aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_aid) was being delivered at [Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md).[^cite_note-217]

#### Kamal Adwan Hospital

Main article: [Kamal Adwan Hospital sieges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamal_Adwan_Hospital_sieges)

On 3 December, the IDF bombed the Kamal Adwan hospital, killing at least four people.[^cite_note-218] Attacks in the vicinity of Kamal Adwan hospital were reported on 5 December.[^cite_note-219] The director of [Kamal Adwan Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamal_Adwan_Hospital) stated Israel had killed two mothers and their newborn babies when Israel targeted its maternity ward.[^cite_note-220] The UN confirmed the killings.[^cite_note-221] Israel raided the Kamal Adwan Hospital.[^cite_note-Tétrault-Farber-222][^cite_note-223] In response, Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus stated WHO was extremely worried for Kamal Adwan's medical staff.[^cite_note-Tétrault-Farber-222]

#### Al-Aqsa Hospital

Further information: [14 October 2024 Al-Aqsa Hospital attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14_October_2024_Al-Aqsa_Hospital_attack)



Relatives of Palestinians who died in Israeli airstrikes gathering around the bodies taken from the morgue of Al-Aqsa hospital in Deir el-Balah

Beginning on 6 January, doctors at [Shuhada al-Aqsa Hospital](75493386.md) reported that Israeli fire was nearing the hospital. Tens of thousands of people were reported to be sheltering at the hospital.[^cite_note-224] British doctor [Nick Maynard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Maynard) stated his medical team was forced to evacuate the hospital as Israeli troops attacked the hospital.[^cite_note-225] [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) and the [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) both also evacuated their medical teams when the IDF dropped leaflets telling nearby residents they were in a "dangerous combat zone."[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-'Deep_concern'_for_patients_and_staff-227] Following a visit to the hospital, the [World Health Organization](33583.md) found 70% of staff and many patients had fled.[^cite_note-228] Doctors reported many patients who were physically unable to move, and that conditions were rapidly deteriorating, with one doctor stating, "A child came in alive, literally burnt to the bone, their hands were contracting. Their face was just charcoal, and they were alive and talking. And we had no morphine."[^cite_note-'Deep_concern'_for_patients_and_staff-227][^cite_note-229]

In an interview with *[NPR](29697232.md)* on 10 January, one American doctor described the situation at Al-Aqsa as the "stuff of nightmares".[^cite_note-230] [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md), the World Health Organization chief, stated, "Three months into this conflict, it is inconceivable that this most essential need — the protection of health care — is not assured."[^cite_note-231] Health professionals and doctors warned of the danger of Al-Aqsa's closure since it was the last remaining hospital in the entirety of central Gaza.[^cite_note-232] On 13 January, the hospital ran out of fuel to power its generators, leading to a blackout that threatened patients.[^cite_note-233] The hospital again ran out of fuel in late-May 2024.[^cite_note-234]

On 1 August 2026, the Israeli military destroyed two warehouses of medical supplies next to the Shuhada al-Aqsa Hospital.[^cite_note-235] Hospital spokesperson Khalil al-Daqran stated that the bombing destroyed many supplies used by the dialysis department and intensive care.[^cite_note-236]

### Evacuation challenges

A man carrying a bloodied and bandaged child.

Medic carrying wounded Palestinian child in Gaza

On 14 October, Israel ordered the evacuation of 22 hospitals in northern Gaza. The WHO described the order as a "death sentence" for the sick and wounded.[^cite_note-237] Doctors across northern Gaza stated they were unable to follow Israel's evacuation order, since their patients, including newborns in the [ICU](6332859.md), would die.[^cite_note-UN_shelters_in_besieged_Gaza-238] Doctors at [al-Quds Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_Hospital) and the [Palestine Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Red_Crescent) reported they received a call from the Israeli army to evacuate the hospital or "bear the consequences".[^cite_note-Wounded_dying-239][^cite_note-240]

On 29 October, the Palestinian Red Crescent reported that it had received warnings from Israeli authorities to immediately evacuate [al-Quds hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_hospital) as it was “going to be bombarded”.[^cite_note-Gdn291023-241] That day, an Israeli airstrike struck 20 metres (65 feet) from the hospital.[^cite_note-242] [Mai al-Kaila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mai_al-Kaila), the [Palestinian Minister of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Palestine\)), stated the Israeli army was not evacuating patients, but rather "forcibly evicting the wounded and patients onto the streets, leaving them to face inevitable death."[^cite_note-243] The director of the Nasr Hospital stated it had been evacuated under threat of Israeli weapons and tanks.[^cite_note-244] Fighting near Al-Quds Hospital halted evacuation efforts.[^cite_note-245]

On 21 November, the World Health Organization stated three hospitals in northern Gaza would be evacuated, meaning there would be no functioning hospitals left in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-246] On 23 November, four patients died in the transfer from northern Gaza to the [Turkish-Palestinian Friendship Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish-Palestinian_Friendship_Hospital).[^cite_note-247] The Red Cross reported its staff were shot at while providing humanitarian support.[^cite_note-248] The [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) announced it would cease coordination with the WHO on patient evacuations following the Israeli arrest of Palestinian doctors.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-251] Staff at the Indonesia Hospital were reportedly ordered by the IDF to evacuate.[^cite_note-252] The head of the Medical Emergency Rescue Committee stated patients and staff at the Indonesia Hospital were evacuated to the [European Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_European_Hospital) in Khan Younis.[^cite_note-253] Hospitals in northern Gaza, including [al-Ahli Arab Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital), were evacuated by the World Health Organization and the Red Cross.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255]

The Health Ministry stated hundreds needed to be evacuated from Gaza to receive medical care.[^cite_note-256] A spokesman for the Gaza crossing authority stated the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing) remained opened for the evacuation of the sick and wounded.[^cite_note-257] On 7 December, the Palestinian Red Crescent stated 60 percent of the wounded in Gaza required urgent medical treatment abroad.[^cite_note-258] Doctors Without Borders stated on 10 December stated that the Israeli army had forced them to evacuate the Martyrs and Beni Shueila clinics, and that healthcare had completely collapsed.[^cite_note-259]

### International aid

| Avatar of Tedros Adhanom GhebreyesusAvatar of Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus | **Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus** (@DrTedros) tweeted: |
| --- | --- |

> Patients were receiving care on the floor, screaming in pain. These conditions are beyond inadequate - unimaginable for the provision of health care. I cannot find words strong enough to express our concern over what we’re witnessing.

December 2, 2023[^cite_note-260]

The [Red Cross](5023832.md) stated Gaza's entire health system was "on its knees".[^cite_note-261] [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) and [UNICEF](19698110.md) issued an "urgent warning" that 130 [premature babies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premature_babies) would die if fuel did not reach Gaza hospitals soon.[^cite_note-Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians-262][^cite_note-aljazeera.com-263] A UN statement signed by five major branches stated deaths could soon "skyrocket" from disease and "lack of healthcare".[^cite_note-264] On 23 October, the [Indonesia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Hospital) ran out of fuel and completely lost power.[^cite_note-265] The World Health Organization warned 46 of Gaza's 72 healthcare facilities had stopped functioning.[^cite_note-266]

On 8 November, the [Ministry of Defence of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Italy\)) announced it was sending a [hospital ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospital_ship) to the coast of Gaza,[^cite_note-aljazeera20231108-267][^cite_note-ilpost20231108-268] in order to guarantee Palestinian civilians access to health services, essential goods and medical drugs.[^cite_note-ilpost20231108-268] The ship, named *Vulcano* ("Volcano"),[^cite_note-ilpost20231108-268] initially had 170 staff members on board, 30 of whom trained for medical emergencies;[^cite_note-aljazeera20231108-267][^cite_note-ilpost20231108-268] 28 more members between physicians, nurses and biologists were set to join the expedition in a later phase.[^cite_note-ilpost20231108-268][^cite_note-270] By early-December, the medical staff started performing emergency surgical operations for injured Palestinian patients who were either at risk of amputation, or waiting to be moved to the Children Hospital in [Doha](26214389.md).[^cite_note-ilpost20231221-269] On 5 February 2024, it was announced that the *Vulcano* had returned to Italy and arrived at the seaport of [La Spezia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Spezia), carrying 60 Palestinian people,[^cite_note-repubblica20240205-271][^cite_note-ilpost20240205-272] 14 of which were children in need of specialized healthcare, although none of them suffered from life-threatening conditions or injuries.[^cite_note-ilpost20240205-272] All of the children were set to be transferred to various hospitals in [Genoa](47332321.md), [Rome](25458.md), [Florence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence), [Bologna](21069333.md) and [Milan](36511.md).[^cite_note-repubblica20240205-271][^cite_note-ilpost20240205-272]

On 9 November, Health Minister [Fahrettin Koca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahrettin_Koca) announced Turkey was prepared to receive Gaza's pediatric cancer patients.[^cite_note-273] On 10 November, the [International Red Cross](5023832.md) stated Gaza's healthcare system had "reached a point of no return."[^cite_note-274] The [Government of Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Jordan) reported Israel had ordered their field hospital to be evacuated and stated they would not comply.[^cite_note-275] Italy and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) stated they were considering establishing a field hospital in Gaza.[^cite_note-ilpost20231221-269][^cite_note-276] A French warship was dispatched as a temporary hospital.[^cite_note-277] On 2 December, Saudi Arabia donated six ambulances to the Palestinian Red Crescent.[^cite_note-278] On 3 December, a [UNICEF](19698110.md) spokesman described Nasser Hospital as a "death zone."[^cite_note-279]

WHO chief [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md) stated fighting in southern Gaza was making it increasingly difficult to run health operations.[^cite_note-280] On 10 December, WHO adopted a resolution to protect healthcare in Gaza, which director-general Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus described as "almost impossible in the current circumstances."[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] On 12 December, WHO pleaded with the Israeli army not to destroy hospitals in southern Gaza.[^cite_note-283] Doctors Without Borders said healthcare conditions in Gaza were akin to conditions during [World War I](4764461.md).[^cite_note-284]

### Additional challenges



Gaza war deaths by month

On 28 October, a [communications blackout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_blackout) meant wounded civilians could not dial emergency services.[^cite_note-285] Ambulances were then evenly geographically distributed to provide "faster access to the injured."[^cite_note-286] A surgeon at [Al-Ahli hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital) stated on 18 November they had run out of blood for transfusions.[^cite_note-287]

Doctors in south Gaza reported a lack of beds and supplies.[^cite_note-288] By 8 December, an estimated 286 health workers in Gaza had been killed by Israel.[^cite_note-289] On 4 December, Doctors Without Borders stated hospitals in southern Gaza were overflowing with wounded patients.[^cite_note-290] The Ministry of Health stated 50,000 people had been wounded since the start of the conflict.[^cite_note-291]

The Red Crescent stated al-Quds was completely out of service on 12 November, as Al-Shifa also stopped receiving patients.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] On 16 November, the [Indonesia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Hospital) completely shut down, leaving 45 patients in need of surgery.[^cite_note-294] [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) stated only four small hospitals in northern Gaza and eight health facilities in southern Gaza were still functioning.[^cite_note-295] On 30 November, WHO chief [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md) stated the health needs of Gaza had increased dramatically, though only one-third of its health facilities were functioning.[^cite_note-296]

On 9 January, Rik Peeperkorn, the WHO representative for Israel and Palestine, stated, "I’ve never seen so many amputees in my life, including among children. This will have such a long-term impact on everything."[^cite_note-297] On 18 January, the United Nations reported patients were dying even while hospitalized because Gaza's remaining hospitals were overwhelmed with tens of thousands of wounded people.[^cite_note-298]

Israel shut the Gaza border crossings on 28 February 2026, the first day of the [war with Iran](82533582.md), interrupting the entry of trucks carrying humanitarian aid and other essential supplies. The closure also stopped patients and injured people from leaving, raising alarm because thousands had been waiting to travel abroad for medical care after Israel's war had devastated Gaza's health system.[^cite_note-howusi-299] After several days of closure, Israel partially reopened the Kerem Abu Salem, or Kerem Shalom, crossing and allowed a small number of trucks carrying aid and essential goods to enter. The reopening had little practical effect, however, as the amount of aid reaching Gaza remained well below the roughly 600 trucks a day needed to meet the population's needs.[^cite_note-howusi-299] Economist Mohammad Abu Jiyab said the US-Israel war on Iran had directly worsened Gaza's economic and humanitarian conditions by cutting imports of aid and commercial goods. This had driven up prices, caused shortages, and weakened humanitarian distribution. Abu Jiyab warned that, if the situation continued, conditions in Gaza would deteriorate further. A UNICEF spokesperson added that prices for some basic goods, including food and cleaning products, had in some cases risen by 200% to 300%.[^cite_note-howusi-299]

## Airstrikes

Main article: [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md)

Further information: [Attacks on refugee camps in the Gaza war](75393092.md) and [Attacks on schools during the Israeli invasion of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_schools_during_the_Israeli_invasion_of_Gaza)

See also: [Dahiya doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahiya_doctrine) and [Roof knocking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roof_knocking)

### October



El-Remal in [Gaza City](55387.md) following an Israeli airstrike, 10 October 2023

In just one week, Israel dropped more than 6,000 bombs on Gaza.[^cite_note-300] Israel's airstrikes were described as a [carpet bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpet_bombing) and "[indiscriminate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack)".[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] By 16 October, airstrikes had killed 2,750 people, including more than 700 children, and wounded nearly 10,000.[^cite_note-drinking_salty_water-47] An additional 1,000 people were missing beneath rubble.[^cite_note-303] On 16 October, Israeli airstrikes destroyed a UNRWA humanitarian aid supply depot.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] The same day, airstrikes destroyed the headquarters of the [Palestinian Civil Defence](49984885.md), the agency responsible for [emergency response services](146330.md), including [firefighting](146303.md) and [search and rescue](172599.md).[^cite_note-306]

On 17 October, Israel conducted intensive airstrikes in southern Gaza, in areas it told residents to seek refuge.[^cite_note-Jobain-refuge-307] An airstrike at a [UNRWA school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_UNRWA_school_airstrike) killed at least six people.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] On 18 October, the Ahmed Abdel Aziz School in Khan Yunis was hit.[^cite_note-310] On the same day, the death toll in Gaza had risen to 3,478.[^cite_note-311] On 19 October, an [Israeli airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Porphyrius_airstrike) hit the [Church of Saint Porphyrius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Porphyrius), where 500 people were sheltering.[^cite_note-312] Israel "pounded" areas in south Gaza it had declared as "safe zones", raising fears amongst residents that nowhere was safe.[^cite_note-313] On 19 October, U.S. officials reported alarm at Israeli comments about the "inevitability of civilian casualties", after it used the [atomic bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki) as historical comparisons for their Gaza campaign.[^cite_note-314]

On 20 October, Israeli continued to bombard south Gaza.[^cite_note-315] IDF spokesman Nir Dinar said, "There are no safe zones".[^cite_note-316] On 21 October, Israel intensified its airstrikes in advance of an expected [ground invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion).[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] On 22 October, Israeli airplanes bombed the areas around the [Al Shifa](21218880.md) and [Al Quds hospitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_Hospital) on a night described as the "bloodiest" of the conflict so far.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] On 23 October, airstrikes killed 436 people in the [al-Shati camp](16296150.md) and southern Khan Younis in just one night.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] On 26 October, Israeli PM [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) stated Israel had "already eliminated thousands of terrorists – and this is only the beginning".[^cite_note-323] On 27 October, WHO stated more than 1,000 unidentified people were buried under rubble.[^cite_note-324]

By 28 October, the Israeli Air Force bombed residential buildings without any prior warning, killing an estimated 50 people per hour.[^cite_note-325] On 29 October, the IDF bombed the area around the Al-Quds hospital.[^cite_note-326] On 30 October, Israel bombed the [Turkish-Palestinian Friendship Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish-Palestinian_Friendship_Hospital).[^cite_note-327] On 31 October, an airstrike on the [Jabalia refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_31,_2023_Jabalia_airstrike) was described as a "massive massacre."[^cite_note-328]

### November



Victim of Israeli airstrike in [Jabalia](18169472.md)

On 3 November, the Health Ministry stated 1,200 children were buried under rubble, 136 paramedics had been killed, and 25 ambulance vehicles had been destroyed.[^cite_note-329] On the same day, Israel [bombed a medical convoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_ambulance_airstrike) outside of al-Shifa hospital.[^cite_note-330] The IDF claimed the ambulance was being used by Hamas, leading [Queen's University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen's_University_at_Kingston) professor Ardi Imseis to state Israel needed to prove its claim.[^cite_note-331] The IDF also bombed the Osama Ben Zaid school.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333] On 4 November, Israel bombed the al-Fakhoora School, killing at least fifteen people.[^cite_note-334] Journalists reported Israel was targeting [solar panels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_panel) and personal generators.[^cite_note-335] On 5 November, Israel bombed and destroyed [Al-Azhar University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_University_%E2%80%93_Gaza).[^cite_note-336] On 6 November, at least eight people died in airstrikes on the [Nasser Medical Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Medical_Complex).[^cite_note-337]

On 8 November, Israel bombed and destroyed the Khalid bin al-Walid Mosque.[^cite_note-338] On 12 November, Israel used [earthquake bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthquake_bomb) on an apartment complex in Khan Younis, killing at least thirteen people.[^cite_note-339] On 13 November, an Israeli airstrike on the Jabalia refugee camp killed thirty people, with Gaza's civil defence team unable to rescue injured people from the rubble due to a lack of equipment.[^cite_note-Responders_cannot_reach-340]

According to sources, both Israeli and Western, Hamas placed military facilities [under schools, hospitals and mosques](43413500.md) throughout Gaza (including [Al-Shifa](21218880.md) and the [Indonesia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Hospital)), or used them as [cover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infantry_tactics#Defensive_tactics) for its fighters.[^cite_note-Tunnel_sources-341] According to *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, there were indications of Hamas' use of hospitals, schools and residential building as early as [2014](43243199.md).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-344] These claims have been disputed, however, including by organizations like [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), [UNRWA](32270.md), and the [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent).[^cite_note-tbsnews20231114-345][^cite_note-Refutations-346] The [Government of Qatar](25190.md) has criticized the lack of either concrete evidence or independent investigations, and both Palestinian and international medical staff have disputed them.[^cite_note-Disputes-347] [Michael Lynk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Lynk) stated Israel's claims were used to prepare "public opinion for the attacks to come".[^cite_note-348]

On 15 November, Gaza's last remaining [flour mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_mill) was hit by an Israeli airstrike.[^cite_note-349] On 17 November, dozens were reported killed after an [airstrike on al-Falah School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Falah_School_airstrike) in the Zeitoun neighborhood, south of Gaza City.[^cite_note-350] The following day, 26 people were killed in an airstrike of a residential building in southern Gaza.[^cite_note-351] A strike on the [Al-Fakhoora school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Fakhoora_school_airstrikes) reportedly killed at least 50.[^cite_note-Hamas_Health_Ministry_Says-352] Deaf, blind, and intellectually handicapped individuals were at particular risk of death by airstrikes.[^cite_note-353] Following Israel's evacuation orders for Palestinians to flee northern Gaza, the IDF intensified its attacks on southern Gaza.[^cite_note-354] It again intensified strikes across Gaza before the [temporary November ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-355] By 26 November, Israel dropped an estimated 40,000 tons of explosives on Gaza.[^cite_note-356]

### December

In the hours following the end of the temporary truce between Israel and Hamas, 109 people were killed by Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-357] On 2 December, the IDF stated it had struck at least 400 locations in Gaza since the pause had ended, including 50 in [Khan Younis](101330.md) in southern Gaza.[^cite_note-358] On 3 December, the [Palestinian Civil Defence](49984885.md) stated the situation "beyond dire" as the organization was unable to rescue many people buried under rubble.[^cite_note-359] The same day, 700 were reported killed in the preceding twenty-four hours.[^cite_note-360] Some individuals were rescued by aid workers after reportedly surviving several days buried underneath rubble.[^cite_note-361] [Robert Pape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Pape) stated, "Gaza will also go down as a place name denoting one of history’s heaviest conventional bombing campaigns."[^cite_note-362] On 8 December, 350 people were reportedly killed in the preceding twenty-four hours.[^cite_note-363] On 9 December, the Palestinian Civil Defence stated it only had one operational rescue vehicle left in the entirety of northern Gaza.[^cite_note-364] On 13 December, a UNRWA school in [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun) was destroyed by an Israeli airstrike.[^cite_note-365] On 14 December, a US intelligence report found half of the bombs dropped on Gaza had been [unguided bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unguided_bomb).[^cite_note-366]

A *[Sky News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News)* analysis found Israel was directly targeting areas that it was telling people to flee to.[^cite_note-367] Experts stated the bombing campaign against Gaza had been the deadliest and most destructive in modern history, with Corey Scher of the [CUNY Graduate Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CUNY_Graduate_Center) stating, "Gaza is now a different color from space."[^cite_note-368] *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* described the Israeli bombing in Gaza as the "most devastating urban warfare in the modern record".[^cite_note-369] Torrential rains caused flooding, which raised fears of disease spread.[^cite_note-370]

### January

On 5 January 2024, evacuees fleeing Israeli attacks in central Gaza stated the situation there was "hell on Earth."[^cite_note-371] One survivor of an Israeli airstrike wrote, "Even though that air strike did not kill us, it destroyed something inside us."[^cite_note-372] On 12 January, the UN Secretary-General for Human Rights stated that at least 319 internally displaced persons were killed and 1,135 injured by Israeli airstrikes while sheltering in UN shelters.[^cite_note-373]

As of January 2024, Israel’s offensive has either damaged or destroyed 70–80% of all buildings in northern Gaza.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] Numerous casualties were reported in an airstrike on a residential building near [Nasser Medical Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Medical_Complex) in [Khan Younis](101330.md), with hospital staff reporting having to bury 40 bodies on the hospital grounds.[^cite_note-376] By 30 January, at least half of all buildings in the entirety of Gaza had been destroyed or damaged.[^cite_note-377]

### February

On 2 February 2024, [UNOSAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOSAT), the UN's satellite centre, found that 69,147 structures, or approximately 30 percent of Gaza's total buildings, had been damaged or destroyed by Israeli airstrikes, shelling, and demolitions.[^cite_note-378] The *New York Times* estimated that at least half of Gaza's buildings had been damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-379] Israeli bombing campaigns intensified in [central Gaza](14704596.md), as displaced people in [Rafah](584373.md) grew fearful of an impending Israeli attack on the city.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381]

## Displacement

Main article: [Gaza Strip evacuations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations)



The line in black represents the IDF's boundary at Wadi Gaza for [evacuation of the northern Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evacuation_of_the_northern_Gaza_Strip)

On 10 October, the United Nations said the fighting had displaced more than 423,000 Palestinians,[^cite_note-displaced1-382] while Israeli airstrikes had destroyed 1,000 homes and rendered 560 housing units uninhabitable.[^cite_note-ap8-43] By 15 October, an estimated 1 million people in Gaza had been displaced, many of them fleeing northern Gaza following Israel's [mandated evacuation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evacuation_of_the_northern_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-collapse-45] Due to continued heavy Israeli bombing in south Gaza, some northern Gazan refugees moved back to Gaza City.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_reporter_films-385] On 19 October, the [UN Office for Humanitarian Affairs](1602284.md) stated 98,000 houses, or 1 in every 4 homes in Gaza, had been destroyed by Israeli bombardments.[^cite_note-386] On 21 October, the UNRWA stated 500,000 people were sheltering in UN facilities, and conditions had grown "untenable".[^cite_note-387] By the end of October this had grown to over 670,000 people.[^cite_note-388] Many others sheltered in hospitals.[^cite_note-389]

By 22 October, the UN Office for Humanitarian Affairs stated 42% of homes in Gaza had been destroyed.[^cite_note-390] By 23 October, an estimated 1.4 million people in Gaza had been left [homeless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeless).[^cite_note-391] On 30 October, the Red Cross stated it would take years to rebuild destroyed homes and infrastructure.[^cite_note-392] On 2 November, UNRWA stated 50 of its buildings and assets had been affected by Israeli strikes, including four shelters.[^cite_note-393] On 6 November, *[Al Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English)* journalist Hani Mahmoud described southern Gaza as a large [concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_camp).[^cite_note-394] As the fighting in Gaza City intensified, the IDF announced a daily four-hour window for residents to move south, leading to thousands fleeing the city.[^cite_note-395] On 10 November, an Israeli spokesman stated 100,000 people had fled northern Gaza in the prior two days.[^cite_note-396] On 11 November, [UNICEF](19698110.md) stated thousands of children in northern Gaza were "hanging on by a thread."[^cite_note-397]

On 12 November, [CARE International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CARE_International) stated, "The journey to the south is incredibly dangerous and hard. Many of those who have made it out have experienced and witnessed terrible suffering."[^cite_note-398] On 14 November, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated, "There is no reliably safe route to evacuate. Satellite imagery confirms fires, military operations, and roadblocks on every conceivable route."[^cite_note-399] By 20 November, satellite imagery showed half of northern Gaza had been destroyed by Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-400] The *[Financial Times](136566.md)* described northern Gaza as a "bombed-out wasteland."[^cite_note-401] Palestinians feared northern Gaza was becoming uninhabitable.[^cite_note-402] By 28 November, the UN estimated 60 percent of all housing in Gaza had been destroyed.[^cite_note-403] The *Financial Times* estimated it would cost billions to rebuild Gaza.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-406]

On 1 December, Israel labelled [Khan Younis](101330.md) a "dangerous combat zone."[^cite_note-washingtonpost20231130-407] It issued a map of numbered zones, dividing the Gaza Strip into hundreds of different districts.[^cite_note-washingtonpost20231130-407] It also issued an [evacuation order in southern Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_Strip_evacuations#Southern_Gaza_evacuations) for residents to move to [Rafah](584373.md).[^cite_note-408] At the same time it issued the evacuation, Israel bombed Rafah.[^cite_note-409] On 4 December, a UN representative stated "another wave of displacement is underway."[^cite_note-410] The UN stated at least four of its shelters in Khan Younis had received evacuation orders from the Israeli military.[^cite_note-411] By 13 December, half of Gaza's population was in Rafah.[^cite_note-412] On 21 December, a *[Financial Times](136566.md)* analysis found Israel had left northern Gaza virtually uninhabitable.[^cite_note-413] On 6 January 2024, UN humanitarian chief Martin Griffiths stated that Gaza had "simply become uninhabitable".[^cite_note-414] More than 85% of Palestinians in Gaza, or around 1.9 million people, were [internally displaced](427408.md).[^cite_note-415]

On 8 January, *Al Jazeera* reported Palestinians in Gaza were using websites like [GoFundMe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GoFundMe) to fundraise the necessary money to enter Egypt via the Rafah Crossing.[^cite_note-416] UNOCHA reported there were as many 15 people living per tent.[^cite_note-UNOCHA-123] On 13 January, the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) reported the infrastructure of [Rafah](584373.md) was at its breaking point, unable to handle the large number of displaced people who'd fled there.[^cite_note-417] [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) stated on 17 January, "You have hundreds of thousands of people living now in the street, living in these plastic makeshift tents, sleeping on the concrete."[^cite_note-418] On 24 January, Israel ordered a large area of Khan Younis to evacuate, affecting three hospitals, 24 United Nations shelters, and more than 500,000 people.[^cite_note-419] In February 2024, Gaza's information office stated the IDF was deliberately burning homes.[^cite_note-420]

## Communications

See also: [Communications in Palestine](26308793.md)

Since the start of the war on 7 October, Gaza has undergone numerous [communications blackouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_blackout).[^cite_note-421] Direct attacks on telecommunications infrastructure by Israel, electricity blockades and fuel shortages have caused the near-total collapse of Gaza's largest cell network providers.[^cite_note-elpais20240129-422][^cite_note-theguardian-423][^cite_note-424] Lack of internet access has obstructed Gazan citizens from communicating with loved ones, learning of IDF operations, and identifying both the areas most exposed to bombing and possible escape routes.[^cite_note-elpais20240129-422] The blackouts have also impeded emergency services, making it more difficult to locate and access the time-critical injured,[^cite_note-elpais20240129-422] and have impeded humanitarian aid agencies and journalists as well.[^cite_note-elpais20240129-422]

On 27 October, Gaza underwent a near total communications blackout after [Paltel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paltel_Group)'s [communication towers](2044496.md) were destroyed in an Israeli attack.[^cite_note-The_Destruction_of_Gaza's_Internet-425] This cut off Gaza from any phone or internet service.[^cite_note-426] As a result, humanitarian groups, including UNICEF, WHO, the [Palestine Red Crescent Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Red_Crescent_Society), Doctors Without Borders, [American Friends Service Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Friends_Service_Committee), Medical Aid for Palestinians, and [ActionAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ActionAid) lost all contact with their staff.[^cite_note-What_aid_groups_have_lost-427] The Palestinian Red Crescent Society stated wounded people would no longer be able to dial Gaza's [emergency number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_telephone_number) for an ambulance.[^cite_note-Phone_Network_Cut-428] The Red Crescent stated it was "deeply concerned" about the ability of [medics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medic) to provide care, stating it had lost all contact with operations room and staff in Gaza.[^cite_note-Phone_Network_Cut-428] On 28 October, [Elon Musk](909036.md) offered to provide humanitarian groups with [Starlink](45111627.md) access, but [Shlomo Karhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shlomo_Karhi) stated Israel would fight it with every "means at its disposal” because Hamas will use it for terrorist activities.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430]

The UN Assistant Secretary-General and Humanitarian Coordinator in the Occupied Palestinian Territory, Lynn Hastings,[^cite_note-431] stated that hospitals and aid operations could not operate without phone lines or internet.[^cite_note-432] The [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists) warned the world was "losing a window into the reality" of the situation in Gaza.[^cite_note-433] [Michael Lynk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Lynk), a former [UN rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur), stated one purpose of the internet blackout was to keep "the world blinded on what's happening."[^cite_note-434] The WHO secretary-general [Tedros Ghebreyesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tedros_Ghebreyesus) stated he was "gravely concerned" by the blackout for the "immediate health risks" it posed to patients and for the safety of WHO staff.[^cite_note-What_aid_groups_have_lost-427][^cite_note-435] In a post on [X](9988187.md), ActionAid wrote the blackout would make it "nearly impossible" for people to seek help, and stated they were "gravely concerned" for "all the people of Gaza."[^cite_note-436]

Marwa Fatafta, policy manager of [Access Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Now), stated that "taking Gaza completely off the grid while launching an unprecedented bombardment campaign only means something atrocious is about to happen."[^cite_note-The_Destruction_of_Gaza's_Internet-425] On 28 October, [Netblocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netblocks) stated Gaza's telephone and internet communications were gradually returning.[^cite_note-437] On 31 October, Fatafta stated the blackout had been used by Israel to cover up potential [war crimes](75113830.md) as they began their [ground invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) and called it a "warfare tactic to induce more pain on the population."[^cite_note-warfare_tactic-438] Paltel announced Gaza had again been cut off from telecommunications and internet service.[^cite_note-warfare_tactic-438] On 3 November, the [BBC](19344654.md) began broadcasting an [emergency radio service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Daily) on [mediumwave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medium_wave) from [Cyprus](5593.md) in attempt to retain communications with Gazan civilians and support information-finding such as "where to access shelter, food and water supplies".[^cite_note-439] On 5 November, internet and telecoms were cut for a third time.[^cite_note-440] Some residents used [eSIMs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESIM) to stay connected to mobile networks.[^cite_note-441] On 16 November, communications in Gaza were cut off for a fourth time.[^cite_note-442] The following day, communications were restored after a limited quantity of fuel was allowed to enter the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-443] On 27 November, Shlomo Karhi stated Musk had agreed not to operate Starlink in Gaza without Israeli approval.[^cite_note-444] On 3 December, PalTel reported another communications blackout.[^cite_note-445] On 14 December, Gaza's communication companies reported the sixth communications blackout since the conflict's start.[^cite_note-446]
