On 20 December, Paltel, [Ooredoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ooredoo), and [Jawwal](21787299.md) reported a communications blackout in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-447] Wounded people unable to call ambulances due to the blackouts reported biking and taking donkey carts to hospitals, while ambulance drivers stated they simply followed plumes of smoke or the sound of explosions.[^cite_note-448] Telecoms went down on 26 December.[^cite_note-449] On 12 January, the ninth communications blackout occurred, cutting off phone and internet access across the Strip.[^cite_note-450] The following day, Jawwal reported an Israeli drone strike had killed two of their engineers attempting to repair the system, noting they had lost a total of 13 employees so far.[^cite_note-451] The communications blackout was the longest of the war.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-454] The tenth blackout occurred on 22 January.[^cite_note-455] In a statement, [UNWRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNWRA) stated, "Disruption of telecommunication services prevents people in Gaza from accessing life-saving information or calling for first responders, and continues to impede humanitarian response."[^cite_note-456] The [Ministry of Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Telecom_and_Information_Technology_of_the_State_of_Palestine) stated on 27 January that Israel had fired live bullets and a tank shell at its telecommunications crews.[^cite_note-457] NetBlocks reported a "high impact" outage on 5 March.[^cite_note-458] Internet was disrupted on 25 May 2024.[^cite_note-459]

By December 2023 200,000 Gazans (approximately 10% of the population) had received internet access through an eSIM provided by [Connecting Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecting_Humanity).[^cite_note-460]

## Humanitarian aid

Main article: [Humanitarian aid during the Gaza war](76066918.md)

Further information: [Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip (2023–present)](75013719.md)

### Initial block on aid

> The amount of aid coming in is a drop in the bucket of Gaza’s humanitarian needs. Before this conflict, about 100 trucks of aid alone entered Gaza \[a day]. So imagine now, with all the hostilities going on, if only this number is coming in.

— Mey al Sayegh, ([Red Cross](9720597.md))[^cite_note-461]

On 9 October, Israel implemented a [complete blockade](75013719.md) on Gaza, preventing the entry of any humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-462] Egypt closed its border to prevent civilians fleeing, but said that it would allow aid to be delivered through the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing).[^cite_note-463] A week later, despite international calls for deliveries, hundreds of tons of aid were stuck on Egypt's side of the border, as Israel bombarded the crossing amid fears of weapons deliveries, and declined to assure Egyptian authorities it would pause airstrikes for civilian aid convoys.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] In Israel, aid to Gaza was reportedly prevented by far-right politicians allied with Netanyahu.[^cite_note-466] On 17 October, the UNRWA stated that there was currently "no water or electricity in Gaza. Soon there will be no food or medicine either".[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468]

### Deliveries to southern Gaza

On 18 October, Israel announced it would allow food, water, and medicine to be delivered to a "safe zone" in west [Khan Younis](101330.md) in southern Gaza, distributed by the United Nations.[^cite_note-Israel_says_it_will_allow_Egypt-469][^cite_note-Joe_Biden_warns_Israel-51][^cite_note-470] Later the same day, US president [Joe Biden](145422.md) announced Egypt agreed to allow 20 trucks with aid to enter Gaza by 20 October.[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472] More than 100 trucks of aid were waiting at the Rafah crossing to enter into Gaza.[^cite_note-473] In a statement, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that without electricity or fuel, however, the provided aid would fail "meeting the needs of Gaza's population".[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-Israel_says_it_will_allow_Egypt-469] On 19 October, US Special Envoy [David M. Satterfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_M._Satterfield) stated the US wanted "sustained" aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-475] The same day, a spokesman for [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated aid distribution in Gaza would be a "big challenge", and the UN reported at least 100 trucks a day of aid were needed.[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477] On 21 October 20 trucks of aid entered Gaza.[^cite_note-478] [António Guterres](471806.md) stated it was not enough to prevent a "humanitarian catastrophe".[^cite_note-479] [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) said the UN was working to develop an "at-scale operation".[^cite_note-480] On 22 October, following the second delivery of trucks, Biden and Netanyahu stated aid would continue to be allowed into Gaza.[^cite_note-481]

### Issues with delivery

See also: [Structural violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Structural_violence)



Trucks with [humanitarian aid](434679.md) waiting to cross from Egypt into the Gaza Strip

On 27 October, Lynn Hastings, the UN's humanitarian coordinator for Palestine, stated Israel opposed the delivery of humanitarian aid to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-Israel_'clearly'_opposes_aid-482] As a result, UN staff would need to risk their own lives if it was determined such aid would be "lifesaving" to people in need.[^cite_note-Israel_'clearly'_opposes_aid-482] [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) stated "soon many more will die" from Israel's blockade.[^cite_note-483]

When asked about Hamas's responsibility for the safety of civilians, Hamas leader [Mousa Abu Marzook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mousa_Abu_Marzook) allegedly replied that "it is the responsibility of the [United Nations](31769.md) to protect them... \[and] it is the responsibility of the occupation to provide them with... services".[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] On 28 October, *The New York Times* reported that Hamas had stockpiled food, water, medicine and sanitary products in underground caches, in amounts that would allow it to continue fighting for several months without resupply.[^cite_note-486] On 12 November, *[Kan 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kan_11)* aired a video taken by a Gazan civilian, that appeared to show Hamas policemen beating civilians approaching a truck carrying humanitarian aid for food, before allegedly taking the supplies for themselves.[^cite_note-487]

Egyptian Foreign Minister [Sameh Shoukry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sameh_Shoukry) stated "Israeli obstacles" were impeding the delivery of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-488] The United Nations announced the communications blackout had brought aid delivery to a "complete halt."[^cite_note-489] On 29 October, a [humanitarian zone](35072890.md) was announced in the Khan Younis area, along with a claim that aid trucks would increase "significantly."[^cite_note-490] On 30 October, [OCHA](1602284.md) director Lisa Doughten pressured the UN Security Council for the use of extra entry points to Gaza, suggesting the [Kerem Shalom border crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) as the only entry equipped for rapidly processing a sufficiently large number of trucks.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] On 13 November, the United Nations announced it no longer had enough fuel to deliver humanitarian aid in Gaza, leading Canadian Foreign Minister [Melanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanie_Joly) to say, "This is not acceptable."[^cite_note-tbsnews20231114-345] On 17 November, the UN suspended aid delivery again due to the shortage of fuel and the cutoff of communications.[^cite_note-493]

### Delivery during temporary ceasefire

The amount of aid entering Gaza increased during the [temporary November ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-494] On 26 November, the largest shipment of humanitarian aid reached northern Gaza since the start of the conflict nearly two months before.[^cite_note-495] Philippe Lazzarini stated the aid entering Gaza was still inadequate.[^cite_note-496] Samer AbdelJaber, a [World Food Programme](33582.md) head, stated people were hungry and desperate.[^cite_note-497] On 28 November, the [White House](33057.md) reported that over 2,000 trucks of aid had entered Gaza since 21 October.[^cite_note-498]

### Resumption of hostilities

Following the resumption of hostilities on 1 December, aid deliveries into Gaza ceased.[^cite_note-499] The IDF informed the [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent) that the entry of trucks was "prohibited, starting from today" until further notice.[^cite_note-500] Later the same day, the United States announced they had requested a reversal of the decision, and Israel stated it was prepared to allow aid at pre-pause levels.[^cite_note-501] On 4 December, the United Nations Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs (OCHA) stated that approximately 100 humanitarian aid trucks and 69,000 litres of fuel entered Gaza on 3 December and 4 December. This was “well below” the on average 170 trucks and 110,000 litres of fuel that were delivered daily during the temporary ceasefire.[^cite_note-502] On 4 December, Lynn Hastings, a UN humanitarian coordinator, stated, "The conditions required to deliver aid to the people of Gaza do not exist" and warned of a "hellish scenario" in which aid delivery was entirely impossible.[^cite_note-503][^cite_note-505] [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) shared a warning on social media from [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths) stating an immediate ceasefire was needed for the UN to continue humanitarian operations.[^cite_note-506] WHO stated Israel shot at its humanitarian relief trucks in [Gaza City](55387.md).[^cite_note-507]

On 15 December, Israel approved reopening of the [Kerem Shalom crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) and announced that the US would be paying to upgrade the [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing).[^cite_note-508] Following a tour of the Rafah crossing, MEP [Barry Andrews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Andrews_\(politician\)) stated he believed Israel was deliberately delaying aid deliveries to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-509] On 19 December, UNRWA headquarters was bombed.[^cite_note-510] UN chief Antonio Guterres stated the way Israel was conducting its offensive was creating obstacles for delivery.[^cite_note-511] On 29 December, Israel fired on a humanitarian aid truck marked with U.N. insignia.[^cite_note-512] Israel announced on 31 December it was prepared to allow aid ships from [Cyprus](5593.md) to enter Gaza.[^cite_note-513] France and Jordan airdropped aid on 5 January.[^cite_note-514] On 11 January, Samer AbdelJaber, a [World Food Programme](33582.md) director, stated the organization had delivered "crucial food assistance to thousands of people facing catastrophic hunger" in [Gaza City](55387.md) for the first time in weeks.[^cite_note-515]

On 13 January 2024, UNOCHA reported the amount of aid Israel was allowing into Israel had significantly deceased since the prior month.[^cite_note-516] Omar Shakir, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md)'s Israel-Palestine director, stated, "This is a deliberate Israeli government policy. Aid is not reaching north Gaza."[^cite_note-517] U.S. senators [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) and [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley) criticized Israel's inspection of humanitarian aid as "arbitrary" and "broken".[^cite_note-518] A joint statement by the heads of [UNICEF](19698110.md), [WHO](33583.md), and the World Food Programme stated the limited quantity of aid arriving in Gaza was unable to prevent the "deadly combination of hunger, malnutrition, and disease".[^cite_note-519] A viral video showed huge crowds of hungry people rushing toward a rumored relief truck.[^cite_note-520] On 16 January, a deal was reached between Israel and Hamas to bring more aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-521] On 19 January, UNOCHA reported that nearly 70% of its aid deliveries to northern Gaza had been denied by Israel.[^cite_note-522] On 29 January, the United Nations reported that Israel had denied 29 aid missions to northern Gaza.[^cite_note-523]

In late-January, at least 15 countries announced they were [suspending funding for UNRWA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNRWA_and_Israel).[^cite_note-524] Philippe Lazarrini, the agency chief, stated, "Our humanitarian operation, on which 2 million people depend as a lifeline in Gaza, is collapsing."[^cite_note-525] The regional director of [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated, "If you stop these trucks, people will die of hunger and very quickly".[^cite_note-526] UNRWA warned that without continued funding, it would be forced to cease operations by the end of February 2024.[^cite_note-527]

### Killing of aid workers

See also: [Societal breakdown in the Gaza Strip during the Gaza war](77392232.md)

From 7 October to 17 December, 135 [United Nations](31769.md) relief workers were killed in the Gaza Strip, making it the deadliest conflict for UN workers in world history.[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529] According to Jeremy Konyndyk, the president of [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), "The U.S. concern about these casualties remains almost purely rhetorical. There is no policy leverage being put behind it whatsoever. Beyond expressing concern and expressing regret, that's where it stops."[^cite_note-530] On 24 February 2024, the U.S. asked Israel to cease killing police in Gaza, stating it was exacerbating the crisis and leading to a "total breakdown of law and order".[^cite_note-531]

## Impact on children



Wounded infant at Al-Shifa Hospital, 11 October 2023

This section is an excerpt from [Children in the Gaza war](75987631.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Children_in_the_Gaza_war\&action=edit)]



Boys salvaging a destroyed building in the Gaza Strip

The [Gaza war](74998519.md) has disproportionately affected children, who make up about 40% of the population of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).[^cite_note-532] In November 2023, [UN Secretary General](162415.md) [António Guterres](471806.md) warned that "Gaza is becoming a graveyard for children. Hundreds of girls and boys are reportedly being killed or injured every day."[^cite_note-533] As the war continued, the toll increased steadily, reaching at least 19,424 children by 3 September 2025.[^cite_note-Children_in_the_Gaza_war_hums42-534]

Within the first month of the war, about 700,000 children in Gaza had been [displaced](477535.md).[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536] A May 2025 estimate reports that 1.9 million people, roughly 90% of Gaza's population, had been displaced by the war. Many had been uprooted repeatedly, in some cases more than ten times.[^cite_note-537] The collapse of essential services created a humanitarian crisis, as families struggled to access food, clean water, and medical care. The ongoing crisis also impacted routine vaccinations, leaving thousands of children at risk of preventable disease. Additional hardships included inadequate shelter, a lack of adequate winter clothing, and the continuing psychological toll on children's mental health.[^cite_note-Children_in_the_Gaza_war_france24live20231213-538][^cite_note-539]

As a result of the war, many children had sustained amputations and other permanent disabilities, as well as psychological trauma. Thousands more suffered from dehydration, malnutrition, diseases linked to the lack of clean water such as [gastroenteritis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gastroenteritis), and respiratory diseases.

In January 2024, two months into the war, the UNICEF deputy director described the situation of children in Gaza as the "most horrific" he had ever seen.[^cite_note-Children_in_the_Gaza_war_unicef.org-540][^cite_note-541] By August 2025, humanitarian conditions for children were reported to have reached an unprecedented level of severity, affecting every aspect of their rights and well-being. Nutritional conditions had become catastrophic, with an officially confirmed famine in three of Gaza's five governorates.[^cite_note-Children_in_the_Gaza_war_hums42-534] A June 2026 UN Independent International Commission of Inquiry report found that Israel had killed approximately 20,000 children and injured 44,000 more since 7 October 2023. The report documented cases of children being killed by single, precise gunshots from snipers and drones, which it said indicated deliberate targeting rather than incidental harm.[^cite_note-Children_in_the_Gaza_war_\:1-542][^cite_note-543]

Breakdown of deaths by age and gender (analysis by professors Michael Spagat and Daniel Silverman), November 2023

[^cite_note-544]

1. Men and boys over 14 (35.3%)

2. Women and girls over 14 (24.1%)

3. Children under 14 (33.8%)

4. Elderly, above 60 (6.80%)

On 22 November, UNICEF reported [unaccompanied children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unaccompanied_minor) had been identified evacuating from northern to southern Gaza by themselves.[^cite_note-545] On 28 November, James Elder, a UNICEF spokesperson, stated wounded children were sheltering outdoors in [car parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parking_lot) and gardens.[^cite_note-546] Elder called the conflict in Gaza a "war on children."[^cite_note-547] Doctors warned children who survived Israeli airstrikes were left with permanent disabilities and trauma.[^cite_note-Gaza's_children_fight_to_survive-548] Aid workers coined the term WCNSF, meaning [Wounded Child No Surviving Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wounded_Child_No_Surviving_Family).[^cite_note-Gaza's_children_fight_to_survive-548] The bodies of some children buried under rubble remained unrecovered.[^cite_note-549] By 3 December, an estimated 6,150 children had been killed in the conflict.[^cite_note-550] In mid-December, the Ministry of Health stated it had run out of vaccinations for children, which would have catastrophic repercussions.[^cite_note-551] The [Euro-Med Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro-Med_Monitor) estimated at least 25,000 children had lost one or both parents.[^cite_note-552] On 19 December, the United Nations stated Gaza was "by far the most dangerous place in the world to be a child".[^cite_note-553] More children were killed in Gaza in two and a half months than the total of children killed in all conflicts around the world in the previous three years combined.[^cite_note-554]

On 21 December, UNICEF reported thousands of children had undergone limb amputations.[^cite_note-555] Save the Children reported 10 children a day in Gaza had lost their limbs, which would result in a lifetime of medical needs.[^cite_note-556] On 28 December, UNOCHA stated 50 percent of all children in the Gaza Strip were experiencing dehydration, malnutrition, respiratory and skin diseases.[^cite_note-557] An UNOCHA representative stated the organization was having difficulty delivering childhood vaccines.[^cite_note-558] On 3 January 2024, UNICEF chief Catherine M. Russell stated many children in Gaza were facing [severe acute malnutrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Acute_Malnutrition).[^cite_note-559] On 5 January, UNICEF found 90 percent of children under the age of two were eating two or fewer food groups a day.[^cite_note-560]

On 6 January 2024, Tanya Haj-Hassan, a doctor with [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md), stated children in Gaza were "dying in every way possible."[^cite_note-561] In some cases, newborns were rescued from under rubble after surviving bombings.[^cite_note-562] A report by Save the Children on 12 January stated children were "enduring unspeakable horrors, including life-changing injuries, burns, disease, inadequate medical care, and losing their parents and other loved ones".[^cite_note-563] On 16 January, doctors reported children weakened by starvation were dying from [hypothermia](146879.md).[^cite_note-564] On 18 January, the deputy executive director of UNICEF stated the suffering of children in Gaza were the "most horrific conditions I have ever seen."[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566] By mid-January 2024, an estimated 10,000 children in Gaza had been killed, with thousands more buried under rubble.[^cite_note-567]

An [Action Aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Aid) coordinator stated on 27 January that children without winter coats were suffering from the cold and rainfall of the winter months, with new commercial products prevented from being brought in.[^cite_note-568] An *Al Jazeera* correspondent reported he had witnessed children sleeping in mud-filled tents.[^cite_note-569] Describing the impact of war on children's mental health, the Save the Children director of humanitarian policy stated the war had "starved and robbed any sense of safety and security".[^cite_note-570] On 29 January, UNICEF reported that 16,000 children were at risk of missing routine vaccinations.[^cite_note-571]

Ahead of an expected Israel invasion of Rafah, Catherine M. Russell stated, "We need Gaza’s last remaining hospitals, shelters, markets and water systems to stay functional. Without them, hunger and disease will skyrocket, taking more child lives."[^cite_note-572]

### Birth and pregnancy

Further information: [Women in the Gaza war § Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Gaza_war#Gaza_Strip)

An estimated 150 babies were born in Gaza per day since the start of the conflict.[^cite_note-573] A pediatric doctor at the Emirati Hospital in [Rafah](584373.md), stated the number of premature babies born in Gaza had risen sharply.[^cite_note-574] [Newborn babies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant) receiving specialized care in the [West Bank](33209.md) were separated from their mothers who were trapped in Gaza.[^cite_note-575] [Oxfam](19698439.md) stated newborn babies were dying from preventable diseases such as infection, [hypothermia](146879.md), diarrhea, and [dehydration](155056.md).[^cite_note-576] By mid-December, parents were struggling to feed newborn babies, as mothers had insufficient nutrition to breastfeed.[^cite_note-577] Newborn babies born during the conflict died in airstrikes, though some were rescued from the rubble.[^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579] A [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) representative stated she had met a woman forced to give birth in the street, and that the baby had died.[^cite_note-580] One woman reported being unable to bathe her newborns more than ten days after their birth, due to the lack of clean water.[^cite_note-581]

On 18 January 2024, [Natalia Kanem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalia_Kanem), the executive director of the [UN Population Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Population_Fund), spoke at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md) at Davos, stating the situation was the "worst nightmare" the UNPF representative had ever witnessed, as there were 180 women giving birth daily, sometimes on the streets of Gaza, as the territory's health system collapsed.[^cite_note-582] On 17 January, [Care International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Care_International) reported a 300 percent increase in the rate of [miscarriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miscarriage) in Gaza since the start of Israel's bombing.[^cite_note-583] [UNICEF](19698110.md) reported on 19 January that 20,000 babies had been born in the Gaza Strip since 7 October.[^cite_note-584] UNICEF described each birth as a baby being "delivered into hell", and stated "humanity cannot allow this warped version of normal to persist any longer."[^cite_note-585] The [UN Women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Women)'s agency reported that since the start of the conflict, two mothers in Gaza had been killed every hour, every day.[^cite_note-586] WHO reported an increase in stress-induced stillbirths.[^cite_note-587]

[Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) stated that women were giving birth in plastic tents, and that those undergoing [C-sections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-sections) were being released within hours.[^cite_note-Displaced_pregnant_women-588] It also reported that women were being turned away from hospitals due to overcrowding, with some forced to birth in public restrooms.[^cite_note-Displaced_pregnant_women-588]

### Premature babies

Main article: [2023–2024 Gaza Strip preterm births](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%932024_Gaza_Strip_preterm_births)



Premature babies at Al-Shifa

The plight of Gaza's premature babies gained global attention.[^cite_note-589] In late October, Gaza's premature babies faced a critical situation as [Medical Aid for Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians) and [UNICEF](19698110.md) warned that 130 infants were at risk of death due to a hospital fuel shortage caused by Israel's siege.[^cite_note-Medical_Aid_for_Palestinians-262][^cite_note-aljazeera.com-263] The lack of fuel led to power outages, endangering premature babies in neonatal intensive care units (NICUs).[^cite_note-abcnews.go.com-126][^cite_note-ABC_News-127] Despite assurances from the IDF to assist in evacuations, the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) reported a lack of evacuation mechanisms, resulting in the deaths of several infants.[^cite_note-washingtonpost.com-186][^cite_note-aje.io-187][^cite_note-590]

The situation escalated in mid-November when Israel launched a [raid on al-Shifa Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_Hospital_siege).[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-191][^cite_note-Israel's_claims_of_providing_incuba-211] Evacuations eventually occurred, facilitated by the [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent), [World Health Organization](33583.md), and [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA), with 31 premature babies moved to southern Gaza, with most then to Egypt.[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-28_Premature_Babies_Evacuated-592] Not all infants were accompanied by their parents, and two died at al-Shifa before the evacuation occurred.[^cite_note-apnews.com-208][^cite_note-593]

The distress extended to Al-Nasr Children’s Hospital, bombed by the IDF in early November, where medical workers had to leave babies in incubators during evacuation.[^cite_note-594][^cite_note-595] Video footage later revealed the aftermath, with five premature babies found dead in their incubators.[^cite_note-Al-Nasr_babies-596] The IDF initially denied responsibility, though an Israel official was heard providing assurances to evacuate the hospital in a released audio.[^cite_note-597][^cite_note-598] In mid-December, a military siege on [Kamal Adwan Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamal_Adwan_Hospital) worsened the situation, as IDF soldiers reportedly prevented staff from supporting 12 babies in intensive care.[^cite_note-599]

## Reactions

A variety of experts, organizations, and countries have labelled Israel's actions against Gaza as [genocidal](75668427.md),[^cite_note-600][^cite_note-601][^cite_note-602] using a variety of aspects of the humanitarian crisis as evidence.[^cite_note-HRW_Blockade-603][^cite_note-Amnesty_International_Blockade-604][^cite_note-605] During a press conference in April 2024, NATO chief [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) stated, "What we see now in Gaza is a humanitarian catastrophe."[^cite_note-606]

### Aid organizations

A [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) video shared by [Amnesty International](18947898.md) head [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard) stated, "This brutal annihilation of an entire populations health system stretches beyond what humanitarian aid can fix."[^cite_note-607] On 4 December, [Red Cross](15487.md) president [Mirjana Spoljaric Egger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirjana_Spoljaric_Egger) visited the Gaza Strip, stating, "the things I saw there are beyond anything that anyone should be in a position to describe."[^cite_note-608] On 10 December, Bushra Khalidi, an expert with [Oxfam](19698439.md), stated the situation was no longer "just a catastrophe, it's apocalyptic."[^cite_note-609]

On 11 December, the presidents of six major humanitarian organizations — [CARE USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CARE_USA), [Mercy Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercy_Corps), the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council), [Oxfam America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfam_America), [Refugees International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_International), and [Save the Children](436885.md) — penned a joint op-ed in *[The New York Times](30680.md)* in which they stated, "We have seen nothing like the siege of Gaza".[^cite_note-610] On 13 December, a group of Israeli human rights and civil society organizations, including [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), penned an open-letter to [Joe Biden](145422.md) urging him to use his influence to help stop the "catastrophic" humanitarian crisis in Gaza.[^cite_note-611] [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) warned the humanitarian crisis was growing increasingly dire and called on the Israeli government to follow [international humanitarian law](1093604.md).[^cite_note-612]

On 27 December, an MSF representative stated, "You absolutely cannot depict this as a humanitarian response: When we cannot guarantee the safety of our teams".[^cite_note-613] Mairav Zonszein, a [Crisis Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Crisis_Group) analyst, stated, "It is clear Israel's war objective is not eradicating Hamas, but eradicating the ability to live in Gaza."[^cite_note-614] B'Tselem reported on 8 January that Israel bore a "positive obligation to allow rapid and unimpeded passage of humanitarian aid, including food" into Gaza.[^cite_note-615] On 11 January, the [Oxfam](19698439.md) Middle East director stated, "The scale and atrocities that Israel is visiting upon Gaza are truly shocking. For 100 days the people of Gaza have endured a living hell."[^cite_note-616][^cite_note-617] On 14 January, the spokesman for the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) (NRC) stated, "Gaza has been made unlivable for military reasons and all civilians are paying the price."[^cite_note-618] The following day, the secretary general of the NRC stated it was the worst humanitarian crises of this century.[^cite_note-619]

In response to a week-long [communications blackout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_blackout) across Gaza in January 2024, the non-profit [Access Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_Now) stated, "It is unconscionable to toy with connectivity amidst unprecedented violence and unfathomable human suffering."[^cite_note-620] On 9 February, an [International Rescue Committee](196452.md) representative stated, "If they aren’t killed in the fighting, Palestinian children, women and men will be at risk of dying by starvation or disease."[^cite_note-621] On 18 February, the heads of eight major humanitarian organizations wrote a joint op-ed, stating, "The speed of the deterioration in Gaza is unprecedented in recent history."[^cite_note-622] On 3 March, the head of the [International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies](9720597.md) stated people in Gaza were in "desperate need of food, water, shelter, and medical care, with no sense of safety".[^cite_note-623] [Civicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civicus) described the situation as "one of the worst humanitarian crises in recent global history".[^cite_note-624] Agnès Callamard, the head of Amnesty, stated, "While the international community is busy pretending Gaza is a humanitarian crisis, Israel continues to violate international law in total impunity".[^cite_note-625]

A letter to the editor of the British medical journal *[The Lancet](463899.md)* warned that the actual death toll in Gaza from both direct and indirect causes could be more than 186,000.[^cite_note-626] Jean-François Corty, a humanitarian doctor and president of the NGO *[Doctors of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9decins_du_Monde)*, said that the Gaza Health Ministry's figures take into account the identified dead, "without taking into account all the dead left under the rubble of the bombardments, or the indirect victims who died because of a lack of care or [access to care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care), or from being transported to a health centre. If you add those who are likely to die of [malnutrition](258979.md) or as a result of [wounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wound) inflicted by Israeli bombardments in the weeks and months to come, because of the risks of [superinfection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superinfection) and because their pathology will be treated late, then yes, the figure of 186,000 deaths mentioned in The Lancet is credible."[^cite_note-627]

#### United Nations



On 27 October, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) passed [Resolution ES-10/21 calling for](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-10/21) an "immediate and sustained" humanitarian truce and cessation of hostilities.

[UNRWA](32270.md) commissioner [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini) described the situation as "bone-chilling," and the [World Health Organization](33583.md) stated that it was "spiraling out of control."[^cite_note-Residents_flee_Gaza_City-628][^cite_note-aj-hosp-629] On October 26, the World Organization declared that Gaza's humanitarian and health crisis had "reached catastrophic proportions."[^cite_note-630] [Martin Griffiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Griffiths), the United Nations head of Humanitarian Affairs, stated the humanitarian crisis in Gaza was the worst he had ever seen in his life, stating, "I don’t say that lightly. I mean, I started off in my 20s dealing with the [Khmer Rouge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmer_Rouge), and you remember how bad that was, the [killing fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_Fields)."[^cite_note-631] Griffith stated it was the worst ever because unlike in other humanitarian crises, the people of Gaza had nowhere to flee.[^cite_note-632] On November 8, UN Human Rights chief [Volker Türk](71721726.md) described the [Rafah Crossing](10475618.md) as "gates to a living nightmare."[^cite_note-633]

On 28 November, UN chief [António Guterres](471806.md) stated, the "humanitarian catastrophe in Gaza is getting worse by the day."[^cite_note-634] On 3 December, WHO secretary-general [Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus](39712205.md) visited [Nasser Medical Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Medical_Complex), stating, "Patients were receiving care on the floor, screaming in pain... I cannot find words strong enough to express our concern over what we’re witnessing."[^cite_note-635] On 6 December, UN chief António Guterres invoked Article 99 of the [Charter of the United Nations](88805.md), stating “We are facing a severe risk of collapse of the humanitarian system."[^cite_note-636] A joint statement by more than 20 UN and humanitarian organizations described the crisis as "amongst the worst we have witnessed".[^cite_note-637]

On 8 December, [UNICEF](19698110.md) spokesperson Thomas White stated, "Civil order is breaking down in Gaza... Society is on the brink of full-blown collapse."[^cite_note-638] Philippe Lazzarini stated, "By any description, it is definitely the worst situation I have ever seen."[^cite_note-639] On 11 December, a UN envoy toured Gaza, leading [Ecuador](9334.md)'s representative to state, "The reality is even worse than what words can speak."[^cite_note-640] On 13 December, UN human rights chief [Volker Türk](71721726.md) stated the crisis was "well beyond breakdown."[^cite_note-641] On 19 December, James Elder, spokesperson of UNICEF said "I'm furious that children who are recovering from amputations in hospitals are then killed in those hospitals."[^cite_note-642]

[Barbara Woodward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbara_Woodward), the British ambassador to the UN, stated many more would die from violence, disease, and famine if action wasn't taken to ease the humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-643] On 31 December, a UNOCHA official stated she was afraid casualties were going to increase exponentially due to "conditions which are literally unbelievable".[^cite_note-644] On 5 January 2024, the United Nations humanitarian chief Martin Griffiths stated, "People are facing the highest levels of food insecurity ever recorded."[^cite_note-645] On 7 January, the UNRWA deputy director reported severe hunger and an almost collapsed healthcare system, stating, "I don't know how much more they can bear before something explodes in the southern part of Gaza".[^cite_note-646]

On 7 January, UN chief Antonio Guterres stated "widespread famine looms" in Gaza,[^cite_note-647] to which the UN special rapporteur for health [Tlaleng Mofokeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlaleng_Mofokeng) stated Gaza was experiencing "deliberate starvation not famine".[^cite_note-648] Speaking at the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) on 12 January, Martin Griffiths stated colleagues who had made it into northern Gaza in recent days had described "scenes of utter horror: Corpses left lying in the road. People with evident signs of starvation stopping trucks in search of anything they can get to survive."[^cite_note-649] The [World Food Programme](33582.md) stated nine out of ten people in northern Gaza were eating less than a meal a day.[^cite_note-650] [UNGA](31957.md) president [Dennis Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Francis_\(diplomat\)) asked, "How much is enough?"[^cite_note-651]

On 20 January, Antonio Guterres stated, "People in Gaza are dying not only from bombs and bullets, but from lack of food & clean water, and hospitals without power & medicine."[^cite_note-652] On 27 January, Martin Griffiths stated, "The people of Gaza have been enduring unthinkable horrors and deprivation for close to four months. Their needs have never been higher."[^cite_note-653] On 30 January, the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) issued a statement saying it was worried about the "dire and rapidly deteriorating humanitarian situation".[^cite_note-654] On 7 February, Martin Griffiths stated, "More than half of Gaza’s population is now crammed in Rafah, a town of originally 250,000 people right on Egypt’s doorstep. Their living conditions are abysmal – they lack the basic necessities to survive, stalked by hunger, disease and death."[^cite_note-655] On 10 February, António Guterres stated an Israeli assault on [Rafah](584373.md) would "exponentially increase what is already a humanitarian nightmare".[^cite_note-656] On 21 February, Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus stated the situation in Gaza was inhumane and described it as a death zone.[^cite_note-657]

On 5 March, UNRWA chief Philippe Lazzarini stated, "Despite all the horrors that Gazans have lived through – and that we have watched – the worst might be yet to come."[^cite_note-658] On 15 March, a representative from the United Nations Population Fund called the humanitarian crisis in northern Gaza a "nightmare".[^cite_note-659] James Elder, a UNICEF official, stated, "The depth of the horror surpasses our ability to describe it," saying that he had seen "skeletal" children in Gaza, the "utter annihilation" of the Strip, and that "Khan Younis, Gaza City barely exist any more".[^cite_note-660] In late-March, António Guterres stated the situation in Gaza was a "non-stop nightmare. Communities obliterated. Homes demolished. Entire families and generations wiped out."[^cite_note-661]

On 22 August, [The UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_UN) declared a full-blown famine in Gaza, with over half a million people facing starvation, [destitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destitution), and preventable deaths.[^cite_note-globalissues20250822-662] Turkey First Lady [Emine Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emine_Erdo%C4%9Fan) appealed to [Melania Trump](1209075.md) for intervention,[^cite_note-663] and another Palestinian journalist was killed by Israeli fire.[^cite_note-664] Protests in Israel demanded a ceasefire, amid mounting pressure on the Netanyahu government.[^cite_note-globalissues20250822-662]

### Israeli

Israeli Major General (ret.) [Giora Eiland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giora_Eiland) compared Israel's situation to that of the United States after [Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Harbor).[^cite_note-Eiland_2023-10-10-665] He argued that if Israel wanted to disarm Hamas, it had "no choice" but to make Gaza a place "that is temporarily or permanently impossible to live in".[^cite_note-666][^cite_note-Eiland_2023-10-10-665] This, he stated, was not a "program for revenge", but a way to get the hostages back.[^cite_note-667] In an op-ed in *[Yedioth Ahronoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yedioth_Ahronoth)* on 19 November, Eiland wrote Israel should not adopt a US narrative that "allows" Israel to only fight against militants.[^cite_note-668] Writing for *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)*, Zvi Bar'el argued the humanitarian crisis was an Israeli military weapon that could be used as a bargaining chip.[^cite_note-669] In a speech posted by the *[Knesset Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset_Channel)*, MK [Tally Gotliv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tally_Gotliv) stated, "Without hunger and thirst among the Gazan population, we will not be able to recruit collaborators".[^cite_note-670]

[Ghassan Alian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghassan_Alian), the head of [COGAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories), stated, "Human animals are dealt with accordingly. Israel has imposed a total blockade on Gaza, no electricity, no water, just damage. You wanted hell, you will get hell."[^cite_note-671] In January 2024, a COGAT representative denied there was a famine in Gaza, stating, "Don't forget that this is an Arab, Gazan population whose DNA is to hoard, certainly when it comes to food."[^cite_note-672] Netanyahu stated Israel was allowing in the absolute "minimum" amount of aid into Gaza and claimed this was preventing a humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-673]

In May 2025, the Knesset held a discussion on the impact of the humanitarian crisis on Israeli public relations. Likud MP [Amit Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amit_Halevi) disagreed with a doctor who said that suffering Palestinian children should be able to receive medical treatment. Halevi claimed, "When fighting a group like this, the distinctions that exist in a normal world don't exist." MP [Limor Son Har-Melech](72187910.md) argued, "No one is starving anyone – stop echoing Hamas lies!" The discussion also featured [Tsav 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsav_9) founder Rashel Twito falsely claiming that blocking humanitarian aid to Gaza led to hostage releases.[^cite_note-674]

A May 2025 poll by the [Institute for National Security Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_National_Security_Studies_\(Israel\)) found that 64.5% of Israelis were unconcerned with the humanitarian and aid situation in Gaza.[^cite_note-675] A June poll by the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem) found that 64% of Israelis saw no need for additional coverage of Gazan civilians, and a plurality of 41% thought that Israeli news channels [Channel 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_12_\(Israel\)) and [Channel 13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_13_\(Israel\)) were biased in favor of the civilians.[^cite_note-676]

### Palestinian

On 4 January 2024, Gaza City mayor [Yahya Al-Sarraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Al-Sarraj) made an urgent appeal to the international community for fuel "to provide the necessary support mechanisms to provide water, sanitation and hygiene services to serve all residents of the Gaza Strip".[^cite_note-677] On 5 January, the [Palestinian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_Expatriates_\(State_of_Palestine\)) stated the humanitarian crisis in Rafah was straining the international community's credibility, stating, "The Israeli government continues its deepening campaign of genocide, comprehensive destruction, and displacement in the Gaza Strip".[^cite_note-678] On 14 January, the Foreign Ministry stated, "The Israeli occupation has turned Gaza into an uninhabitable place, committed horrific crimes, and forcibly displaced approximately 2 million people".[^cite_note-679] The Ministry stated Israel was claiming self-defense to justify making Gaza uninhabitable.[^cite_note-680]

### United States

Ten days into the war, the United States UN representative [Linda Thomas-Greenfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Thomas-Greenfield) vetoed a [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) resolution that would have condemned all violence against civilians in the war and urged humanitarian aid to Gaza. She said it was too early to craft a resolution while diplomacy was underway and criticized the one proposed for not mentioning Israel's right to self-defense.[^cite_note-681] In a *[Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_Journal)* op-ed, conservative American commentator [Daniel Henninger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Henninger) speculated Hamas wanted to create a humanitarian crisis for publicity purposes.[^cite_note-682]

US Vice-President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) said on 2 December that "too many innocent Palestinians have been killed" and that "international humanitarian law must be respected". She said that at the end of the conflict there must be "No forcible displacement, no reoccupation, no siege or blockade, no reduction in territory, and no use of Gaza as a platform for terrorism".[^cite_note-683] Ten days later, US President Biden warned that Israel was losing international support because of its "indiscriminate bombing" of Gaza.[^cite_note-684]

In early March 2024, the United States began [airdrops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrops) of humanitarian aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-685] Following an incident in which an airdrop of aid killed 5 Gazans and injured several others, the [US Central Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Central_Command)[^cite_note-686] and an unnamed U.S. official on 8 March rejected reports that their airdrops were to blame.[^cite_note-687] The US also began work on opening a 'maritime aid corridor' and building the [Gaza floating pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_floating_pier) so aid could be delivered by sea.[^cite_note-688] On 4 March, US Vice-President Harris called for "an immediate ceasefire" because of "the immense scale of suffering in Gaza". She said Israel must let more aid into Gaza and was imposing "unnecessary restrictions".[^cite_note-689] US Senator [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) had said that "political decisions by the Netanyahu coalition" were delaying the delivery of aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-690]

The US put forward a draft UN Security Council resolution which stated the "imperative" for "an immediate and sustained ceasefire", facilitating aid delivery, and supporting ongoing talks between Israel and Hamas, linked to the release of hostages. On 22 March it was vetoed by Russia and China.[^cite_note-691] On 25 March, the US abstained on a [UN Security Council ceasefire resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2728), allowing it to pass.[^cite_note-692] On 26 March, the U.S. Secretary of Defense called the situation in Gaza a "human catastrophe".[^cite_note-693]

US [Republican](32070.md) Congressman and former aide to [Donald Trump](4848272.md), [Max Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Miller_\(politician\)), speaking at [Fox News](11121.md) stated that Palestine is "about to get eviscerated... to turn that into a parking lot." He has previously called on the Biden administration "to get out of Israel's way and to let Israel do what it needs to do best". He said there should be "no rules of engagement" during Israel's bombardment of Gaza.[^cite_note-694] Miller also questioned the accuracy of the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md)'s claim that 10,000 people have been killed in Gaza, saying that he believes many of those killed have been "Hamas terrorists", not innocent civilians, and said the United States does not "trust an entity that puts munitions in mosques, and churches and in hospitals."[^cite_note-695]

Former Republican [Representative](19468510.md) [Michele Bachmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michele_Bachmann) appearing in December in [The Charlie Kirk Show](53245776.md) stated "So, it's time that Gaza ends. The two million people who live there – they are clever assassins. They need to be removed from that land. That land needs to be turned into a national park. And since they're the voluntary mercenaries for Iran, they need to be dropped on the doorstep of Iran. Let Iran deal with those people." She received a round of applause from the audience, while Kirk replied "I look at Israel and Israel says we never want another person into our country that doesn't share our values," Kirk said. "They said they don't want refugees. They don't want any of these people. I want American immigration policy to be like that."[^cite_note-696][^cite_note-697]

Republican Representative, [Brian Mast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Mast), compared all Palestinians to Nazis in November on the House floor.[^cite_note-698] On January 31, 2024, Mast also said that Palestinian babies are not innocent civilians but "terrorists" who should be killed, that more infrastructure in Gaza needs to be destroyed, and that "It would be better if you kill all the terrorists and kill everyone who are supporters."[^cite_note-699]

In an interview with [Fox News](11121.md) on March 5, 2024, the former president and presumptive Republican presidential candidate [Donald Trump](4848272.md) stated that [Joe Biden](145422.md) "dumped Israel" due to being overly influenced by pro-Palestinian protests, that "The Democrats are very bad for Israel," that he supports Israel’s ongoing offensive on Gaza in which Israel has to "finish the problem", and that the Biden administration "got soft", which commentators has viewed as a call to continue and "double down" on genocidal acts. Trump's campaign also said that, if elected again, he would bar Gaza residents from entering the U.S. as part of an expanded travel ban.[^cite_note-700]

In a [town hall](383746.md) meeting on March 25, 2024, the [Republican](32070.md) [US House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_House) representative [Tim Walberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Walberg) of [Michigan](18859.md) stated that Palestinian civilians should have nuclear weapons used against them, "like [Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagasaki) and [Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima)" (the Japanese cities where the US dropped atomic bombs at the end of [World War Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_Two), killing hundreds of thousands of people) in order to "Get it over quick."[^cite_note-TimWalbergNuclearGenocide1-701][^cite_note-TimWalbergNuclearGenocide2-702][^cite_note-TimWalbergNuclearGenocide3-703]

A group of eight [Democrat](5043544.md) Senators led by [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders), [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley), and [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen) issued an official letter to President Joe Biden, calling on him to "enforce federal law" by requiring Israel's Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu "to stop restricting humanitarian aid access to Gaza or forfeit U.S. military aid to Israel" as "The severe humanitarian catastrophe unfolding in Gaza is nearly unprecedented in modern history" and "The United States should not provide military assistance to any country that interferes with U.S. humanitarian assistance." They cited the 1961 [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act), which states that "no assistance" shall be provided under that law or the [Arms Export Control Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_Export_Control_Act) to any country that restricts, directly or indirectly, the transport or delivery of U.S. humanitarian assistance. "Stopping American humanitarian aid is in violation of the law. That should be clear. No more money to [Netanyahu's](56472.md) war machine to kill Palestinian children," Sanders said.[^cite_note-704]

### Other states

On 8 January 2024, Jordanian foreign minister [Ayman Safadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_Safadi) condemned Israel's actions in Gaza, stating, "The Israeli aggression on Gaza has exceeded all humanitarian, legal and moral limits".[^cite_note-705]

On 9 January 2024, British Foreign Secretary [David Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Cameron) admitted he is "worried" that Israel has "taken action that might be in breach of international law", saying he wanted Israel to restore water supplies to Gaza.[^cite_note-706] Cameron stated on 26 January, "The scale of suffering in Gaza is unimaginable".[^cite_note-707] [Sarah Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Champion), a Labor MP, stated, "I find it utterly wicked and immoral that international conventions \[in Gaza] are not being respected, particularly when it comes to medical facilities".[^cite_note-708] Chinese foreign minister [Wang Yi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi_\(politician\)) stated, "We cannot allow this humanitarian disaster to continue",[^cite_note-709] and described it as "a tragedy for humankind and a disgrace for civilization".[^cite_note-710] [Yousef Al Otaiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yousef_Al_Otaiba), the UAE ambassador to the United States, called for a ceasefire, writing, "Half of the people are [starving](75828824.md). The medical system has [collapsed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse). Safe water is scarce. An impending [Israeli offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) will displace millions."[^cite_note-711]

Speaking about [Rafah](584373.md), a German government spokesperson stated on 19 February 2024 that although the full scope of the crisis was difficult to assess, "Everyone recognizes that it is catastrophic."[^cite_note-712] [William, Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales) stated, "There is a desperate need for increased humanitarian support to Gaza."[^cite_note-713] On 3 March, France’s Foreign Minister [Stéphane Séjourné](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St%C3%A9phane_S%C3%A9journ%C3%A9) stated, "The humanitarian situation in Gaza has been catastrophic for several weeks, if not several months. And this is creating indefensible and unjustifiable situations for which the Israelis are accountable".[^cite_note-714] On 26 March, the German foreign minister [Anna Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Baerbock) stated, "The humanitarian situation in Gaza is hell".[^cite_note-715]

### Resolution efforts

In a call on 20 November, Chinese President [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) and French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) discussed measures to avoid the humanitarian crisis from getting worse.[^cite_note-716] In Riyadh, Saudi Arabia on November 11, 2023, the [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) and the [Arab League](52625.md) held an [emergency meeting on the Gaza humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Islamic_extraordinary_summit_2023) created by the war.[^cite_note-717] On 5 December, Cyprus President [Nikos Christodoulides](58652814.md) visited Egypt and Jordan in attempt to establish a humanitarian aid corridor to Gaza.[^cite_note-718] The UN appointed [Sigrid Kaag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigrid_Kaag) in the newly created position of senior humanitarian and reconstruction coordinator for Gaza.[^cite_note-719]

On December 6, [United Nations](31769.md) [secretary-general](162415.md) [António Guterres](471806.md) invoked Article 99 of the [UN Charter](88805.md), that is: "The Secretary-General may bring to the attention of the Security Council any matter which in his opinion may threaten the maintenance of international peace and security."[^cite_note-720][^cite_note-721] Guterres cited the situation as a threat to "international peace and security" and "a severe risk of collapse of the humanitarian system" which he argued have irreversible impacts for Palestinians.[^cite_note-722] By invoking Article 99, Guterres is pushing the [Security Council](31956.md) to call for a cease fire.[^cite_note-723]

### Refugees

At the beginning of the war, [Egypt](8087628.md) announced it was closing the [Rafah Crossing](10475618.md) to Gaza, one of only three exit points along the Gaza border.[^cite_note-724][^cite_note-725] Egypt cited fears about [permanent displacement](477535.md) and a possible [refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugee_crisis), particularly if Israel refused to allow the refugees back into Gaza after the war.[^cite_note-726][^cite_note-728] Israeli PM [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) sought to convince Egypt to accept Gazan refugees.[^cite_note-729]

[Jordan](7515964.md) also expressed reluctance to receive Palestinian refugees.[^cite_note-thehill20231018-730] King [Abdullah II of Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_II_of_Jordan) warned Israel against pushing Palestinians into Jordan, emphasizing the need to address the humanitarian situation within both Gaza and the [West Bank](33209.md).[^cite_note-731]

In Europe, [Humza Yousaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humza_Yousaf), the [First Minister of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Minister_of_Scotland), urged the international community to establish a refugee program for Gaza.[^cite_note-732][^cite_note-Willing_to_be_a_place_of_sanctuary-733] Yousaf stated Scotland was ready to offer sanctuary to refugees, and called on the UK to create a resettlement scheme.[^cite_note-Willing_to_be_a_place_of_sanctuary-733][^cite_note-734] Foreign Minister [Hanke Bruins Slot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanke_Bruins_Slot) stated the Netherlands was discussing the possibility of accepting sick and wounded Palestinian children into the country.[^cite_note-735]

In the US, left-wing politicians, including Representatives [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez) and [Jamaal Bowman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaal_Bowman), called for the acceptance of Gazan refugees.[^cite_note-thehill20231018-730] Right-wing politicians, such as former-President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and Governor [Ron DeSantis](36729527.md), both argued for barring admittance of any refugees from Gaza.[^cite_note-thehill20231018-730] Former US ambassador to the UN, [Nikki Haley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Haley), said that [Palestinian refugees](74681.md) from Gaza should be accepted by Middle Eastern countries.[^cite_note-736]

Most cultivated fields have been destroyed, and accessing open areas during the war is dangerous in any case. Bakeries, factories and food warehouses have been bombed or shut down due to lack of basic supplies, fuel and electricity."<sup>[\[26\]](#cite_note-Gaza_Strip_famine_Israel_is_starving-27)</sup>

[^cite_note-270]: &#x20;The [Italian Joint Operations Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Operations_Command_\(Italy\)) also announced a plan to build an [Armed Forces](14705.md)-owned [field hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_hospital) within the Gaza Strip, although the designation of the specific site was subject to the assurance of basic safety conditions in the chosen area.<sup>[\[261\]](#cite_note-ilpost20231108-268)</sup><sup>[\[262\]](#cite_note-ilpost20231221-269)</sup> The Ministry of Defence authorized medical staff from foreign countries and international organizations to join the personnel of both the hospital ship and the field hospital;<sup>[\[261\]](#cite_note-ilpost20231108-268)</sup><sup>[\[262\]](#cite_note-ilpost20231221-269)</sup> as a result, six between physicians and nurses from the [Qatar Armed Forces](25194.md), as well as a medical team affiliated to the [Francesca Rava Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesca_Rava_Foundation?action=edit\&redlink=1), joined the staff of the ship.<sup>[\[262\]](#cite_note-ilpost20231221-269)</sup> However, due to the constant escalation of the war in the Gaza Strip, the *Vulcano* was authorized to change its path following a [NATO](21133.md) meeting, and arrived in [Al'Arish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al'Arish), [Egypt](8087628.md) on 3 December.

Retrieved 9 April 2025. Our report leaves no room for ambiguity. A genocide is unfolding before our eyes. Failing to act now—failing to put an end to this atrocity crime — will tear apart the very foundation of the international rule of law we have collectively built to protect peace, security, and the well-being of all. [^cite_note-genocide-6]: &#x20; [Dumper & Badran 2024](#CITEREFDumperBadran2024), p. 2: "In this context we should not overlook the latest turning point in the history of Palestine – the attack by Hamas on 7th October 2023 on Israeli settlements adjacent to Gaza and the subsequent genocidal war that the state of Israel has carried out in the Gaza strip" [Speri 2024](#CITEREFSperi2024) [Narea 2024](#CITEREFNarea2024) [Albanese 2024](#CITEREFAlbanese2024), p. 1: "By analysing the patterns of violence and Israeli policies in its onslaught on Gaza, the present report concludes that there are reasonable grounds to believe that the threshold indicating that Israel has committed genocide has been met" [Amnesty International 2024](#CITEREFAmnesty_International2024), p. 13: "This report focuses on the Israeli authorities' policies and actions in Gaza as part of the military offensive they launched in the wake of the Hamas-led attacks on 7 October 2023 while situating them within the broader context of Israel's unlawful occupation, and system of apartheid against Palestinians in Gaza, the West Bank, including East Jerusalem, and Israel. It assesses allegations of violations and crimes under international law by Israel in Gaza within the framework of genocide under international law, concluding that there is sufficient evidence to believe that Israel's conduct in Gaza following 7 October 2023 amounts to genocide." [Traverso 2024](#CITEREFTraverso2024), p. 8: "The only normative definition we have, codified at the United Nations Genocide Convention of 1948, accurately describes the current situation in Palestine ... describes exactly what is happening in Gaza today" ["One year of denouncing the genocide of Palestinians in Gaza"](https://www.fidh.org/en/region/north-africa-middle-east/israel-palestine/one-year-of-denouncing-the-genocide-of-palestinians-in-gaza). *[International Federation for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_for_Human_Rights)*. 12 December 2024. Retrieved 4 June 2025. One year ago, the FIDH International Board, its governing body elected by all its member organisations, recognised, after extensive debate and examination, that Israel was carrying out genocide against the Palestinian people in Gaza [B'Tselem 2025](#CITEREFB'Tselem2025), p. 86: "The review presented in this report leaves no room for doubt: since October 2023, the Israeli regime has been responsible for carrying out genocide against the Palestinians in the Gaza Strip. Killing tens of thousands of people; causing bodily or mental harm to hundreds of thousands more; destroying homes and civilian infrastructure on a massive scale; starvation, displacement, and denying humanitarian aid—all this is being perpetrated systematically, as part of a coordinated attack aimed at annihilating all facets of life in the Gaza Strip."

[^cite_note-MotherJonesTotalDeaths-115]: &#x20;Hurwitz, Sophie (8 October 2024). ["Report: In One Year, More Than 100,000 Deaths in Gaza—Aided by \$17.9 Billion From the US"](https://www.motherjones.com/politics/2024/10/us-israel-funding-gaza-palestine-deaths-october-100000-17-billion/). *[Mother Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Jones_\(magazine\))*. Retrieved 17 October 2024. Brown University's Costs of War Project calculated "the money that's spent on war, and the toll on human lives" after a year of war in Gaza. The numbers are staggering.

[^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-116]: &#x20;Stamatopoulou-Robbins, Sophia (7 October 2024). ["The Human Toll: Indirect Deaths from War in Gaza and the West Bank, October 7, 2023 Forward"](https://watson.brown.edu/costsofwar/files/cow/imce/papers/2023/2024/Costs%20of%20War_Human%20Toll%20Since%20Oct%207.pdf) (PDF). [Watson Institute for International and Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watson_Institute_for_International_and_Public_Affairs), [Brown University](4157.md). Retrieved 17 October 2024. In addition to killing people directly through traumatic injuries, wars cause "indirect deaths" by destroying, damaging, or causing deterioration of economic, social, psychological and health conditions. Most expansively, this report describes the causal pathways that can be expected to lead to far larger numbers of indirect deaths. These deaths result from diseases and other population-level health effects that stem from war's destruction of public infrastructure and livelihood sources, reduced access to water and sanitation, environmental damage, and other such factors. This report builds on a foundation of previous Costs of War research for its framework and methodology in covering the most significant chains of impact, or causal pathways, to indirect war deaths in Gaza and the West Bank. Unlike in combat, these deaths do not necessarily occur immediately or in the close aftermath of the battles which many observers focus on. While it will take years to assess the full extent of these population-level health effects, they will inevitably lead to far higher numbers of deaths than direct violence.

[^cite_note-GazaHealthcareLettersEstimatedDeaths-117]: &#x20;["Appendix to letter of October 2, 2024 re: American physicians observations from the Gaza Strip since October 7, 2023"](https://web.archive.org/web/20250103061219/https://static1.squarespace.com/static/66e083452b3cbf4bbd719aa2/t/66fcd754b472610b6335d66f/1727846228615/Appendix+20241002.pdf) (PDF). *gazahealthcareletters.org*. Gaza Healthcare Letters. 2 October 2024. Archived from [the original](https://static1.squarespace.com/static/66e083452b3cbf4bbd719aa2/t/66fcd754b472610b6335d66f/1727846228615/Appendix+20241002.pdf) (PDF) on 3 January 2025. Retrieved 17 October 2024. These are the most conservative estimates of the death toll that can be made with the given available data as of September 30, 2024. It is highly likely that the real number of deaths in Gaza from this conflict is far higher than this most conservative estimate. Without an immediate ceasefire the death toll will only continue to mount, especially among young children.
